## Life Saving Equipment Regulations {#life-saving-equipment-regulations .Title-of-Act}

[C.R.C.]{.abbr title="Consolidated Regulations of Canada"}, c. 1436
:::

[CANADA SHIPPING ACT, 2001](/eng/acts/C-10.15)

Regulations Respecting Life Saving Equipment
::::

::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::: section
## [Short Title]{.HTitleText1} {#h-517232 .Part}

**[[1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-1 .sectionLabel}** These Regulations may be
cited as the [Life Saving Equipment
Regulations](/eng/regulations/C.R.C.,_c._1436).

## [Interpretation]{.HTitleText1} {#h-517234 .Part}

- **[[2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-2 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  definitions in this subsection apply in these Regulations.

  [[accredited service technician]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[accredited service technician]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}[\[Repealed,
      SOR/2023-257, s. 430\]]{.Repealed}

  [[Act]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[Act]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the [Canada Shipping
      Act](/eng/acts/S-9). ([Loi]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[approved]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[approved]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}, in respect of survival craft or
      equipment, means that the prototype of the survival craft or
      equipment has been approved by the Board as having met the
      construction and performance standards set out or referred to in
      these Regulations. ([homologué]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[approved boat]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[approved boat]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a boat that was
      recognized and approved as an [[approved
      boat]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} under these Regulations as they read
      prior to April 28, 1996. ([embarcation approuvée]{.DefinedTermLink
      lang="fr"})

  [[certified]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[certified]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means certified by the
      Board. ([autorisé]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[Class II EPIRB]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[Class II EPIRB]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means an emergency position
      indicating radiobeacon. ([RLS de classe II]{.DefinedTermLink
      lang="fr"})

  [[complement]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[complement]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means

      - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in respect of a ship, the number of persons
        authorized to be carried under the inspection certificate issued
        for the ship; and

      - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in respect of a survival craft, the number of
        persons approved to be carried on the survival
        craft. ([chargement en personnes]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[existing ship]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[existing ship]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a Canadian ship that
      is not a new ship. ([navire existant]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[float-free device]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[float-free device]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a device that
      enables a survival craft to be released automatically from a
      sinking ship and to be ready for use. ([dispositif à dégagement
      libre]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[free-fall launching]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[free-fall launching]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a method of
      launching a survival craft by which the craft, with its complement
      and equipment on board, is released and allowed to fall free into
      the water without any restraining equipment. ([mise à l'eau en
      chute libre]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[Gulf of St. Lawrence]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[Gulf of St. Lawrence]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the area
      bounded on the east by the west coast of the Island of
      Newfoundland, on the north by a line joining Flowers Island and
      Point Amour, Newfoundland, and on the southeast by a line joining
      Port aux Basques, Newfoundland, and Sydney, Nova Scotia. ([golfe
      du Saint-Laurent]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[IMO Resolution MSC.81(70)]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[IMO Resolution MSC.81(70)]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the annex
      to International Maritime Organization Resolution MSC.81(70),
      Revised Recommendation on Testing of Life-Saving Appliances, as
      amended from time to time. ([résolution MSC.81(70) de
      l'OMI]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[inflatable survival equipment]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[inflatable survival equipment]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means an
      inflatable life raft, an inflatable rescue platform or a marine
      evacuation system and includes

      - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a container for the inflatable life raft, the
        inflatable rescue platform or the marine evacuation system;

      - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a hydrostatic release unit; and

      - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a release hook for an inflatable davit-launched
        life raft. ([équipement de sauvetage gonflable]{.DefinedTermLink
        lang="fr"})

  [[inland voyage]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[inland voyage]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} has the same meaning as in
      section 1 of the [Vessel Safety Certificates
      Regulations](/eng/regulations/SOR-2021-135). ([voyage en eaux
      internes]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[inspector]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[inspector]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a steamship inspector
      appointed under section 301 of the
      Act. ([inspecteur]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[launching device]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[launching device]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a device for
      launching a survival craft safely to the water from its stowed
      location. ([dispositif de mise à l'eau]{.DefinedTermLink
      lang="fr"})

  [[length]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[length]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means

      - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a ship that is registered under
        the Act or is required by the Act to be registered, the length
        set out in the ship's certificate of registry; or

      - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a ship that is not required by
        the Act to be registered, the horizontal distance between
        perpendiculars erected at the extreme ends of the outside of the
        hull. ([longueur]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[LSA Code]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[LSA Code]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the annex to International
      Maritime Organization Resolution MSC.48(66), International
      Life-Saving Appliance Code, as amended from time to
      time. ([Recueil LSA]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[major conversion]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[major conversion]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}, in respect of a ship,
      means a change that

      - [(a)]{.lawlabel} substantially alters the dimensions or carrying
        capacity of the ship; or

      - [(b)]{.lawlabel} alters the type of the ship. ([transformation
        importante]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[marine evacuation system]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[marine evacuation system]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means life saving
      equipment that consists of one or more inflatable life rafts, a
      slide or chute as a means of embarkation into the inflatable life
      rafts and, in the case of a system with more than one life raft,
      an inflatable rescue platform. ([dispositif d'évacuation en
      mer]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[near coastal voyage, Class 1]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[near coastal voyage, Class 1]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} has the same
      meaning as in section 1 of the [Vessel Safety Certificates
      Regulations](/eng/regulations/SOR-2021-135). ([voyage à proximité
      du littoral, classe 1]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[near coastal voyage, Class 2]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[near coastal voyage, Class 2]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} has the same
      meaning as in section 1 of the [Vessel Safety Certificates
      Regulations](/eng/regulations/SOR-2021-135). ([voyage à proximité
      du littoral, classe 2]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[new ship]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[new ship]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a Canadian ship that is

      - [(a)]{.lawlabel} with respect to a Safety Convention ship,

        - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a ship the keel of which was laid on or after
          July 1, 1986,

        - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} a ship the keel of which was laid before
          July 1, 1986 and that underwent a major conversion on or after
          July 1, 1986, or

        - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} a ship that was registered in Canada on or
          after July 1, 1986; and

      - [(b)]{.lawlabel} with respect to a ship that is not a Safety
        Convention ship,

        - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a ship the keel of which was laid on or after
          April 28, 1996,

        - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} a ship that underwent a major conversion on
          or after April 28, 1996, or

        - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} a ship that was registered in Canada on or
          after April 28, 1996. ([navire neuf]{.DefinedTermLink
          lang="fr"})

  [[person]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[person]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a person who is one year of
      age or over. ([personne]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[pyrotechnic distress signal]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[pyrotechnic distress signal]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a rocket
      parachute flare, a hand flare or a buoyant smoke signal. ([signal
      de détresse pyrotechnique]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[recovery arrangements]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[recovery arrangements]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}, in respect of a
      ship, means equipment for

      - [(a)]{.lawlabel} hoisting a survival craft safely from the
        water; and

      - [(b)]{.lawlabel} where the survival craft serves the ship,
        returning the survival craft to its stowed location. ([moyens de
        récupération]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[rescue boat]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[rescue boat]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a vessel designed to be
      used for rescuing persons in distress and marshalling survival
      craft. ([canot de secours]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[rigid]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[rigid]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}, in respect of a life raft, an
      emergency boat or a rescue boat, means constructed of rigid
      materials or a combination of rigid materials and inflatable
      compartments and not relying wholly on inflatable compartments for
      buoyancy and form. ([rigide]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[SART]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[SART]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a radar transponder designed
      for search and rescue purposes. ([répondeur SAR]{.DefinedTermLink
      lang="fr"})

  [[sea area A1]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[sea area A1]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} has the meaning assigned in
      Chapter IV of the Safety Convention. ([zone océanique
      A1]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[sheltered waters voyage]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[sheltered waters voyage]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} has the same
      meaning as in section 1 of the [Vessel Safety Certificates
      Regulations](/eng/regulations/SOR-2021-135). ([voyage en eaux
      abritées]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[short international voyage]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[short international voyage]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means an
      international voyage

      - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in the course of which a ship is not more than
        200 nautical miles from a port or place in which the passengers
        and crew could be placed in safety; and

      - [(b)]{.lawlabel} that does not exceed 600 nautical miles in
        length between the last port of call in the country in which the
        voyage begins and the final port of destination. ([voyage
        international court]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[survival craft]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[survival craft]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a lifeboat, a rescue
      boat, an emergency boat, a suitable boat, a buoyant apparatus, a
      life raft or an inflatable rescue platform. ([bateau de
      sauvetage]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[tanker]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[tanker]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a cargo ship constructed or
      adapted for the carriage in bulk of liquid cargoes of a flammable,
      toxic or hazardous nature and includes a chemical tanker and a
      liquefied gas tanker. ([navire-citerne]{.DefinedTermLink
      lang="fr"})

  [[tow-boat]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[tow-boat]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a ship engaged in towing
      another ship or a floating object astern or alongside or in
      pushing another ship or a floating object ahead. ([bâtiment
      remorqueur]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[TP 14475]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[TP 14475]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the publication entitled
      Canadian Life Saving Appliance Standard, published by the
      Department of Transport in December 2023. ([TP
      14475]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[unlimited voyage]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[unlimited voyage]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} has the same meaning as
      in section 1 of the [Vessel Safety Certificates
      Regulations](/eng/regulations/SOR-2021-135). ([voyage
      illimité]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[VHF coverage area]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[VHF coverage area]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means

      - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the Great Lakes;

      - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the Saguenay River downstream from Chicoutimi;

      - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the St. Lawrence River as far seaward as a
        straight line drawn

        - [(i)]{.lawlabel} from Cap-des-Rosiers to West Point, Anticosti
          Island, and

        - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} from Anticosti Island to the north shore of
          the St. Lawrence River along the meridian of longitude 63°
          west;

      - [(d)]{.lawlabel} Puget Sound, State of Washington, U.S.A.; or

      - [(e)]{.lawlabel} all waters that are within a coverage radius of
        a Canadian Coast Guard or U.S. Coast Guard radio station
        providing a continuous maritime mobile distress and safety
        service on frequency 156.8 MHz (channel 16). ([zone
        VHF]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2023-257, s. 430\]]{.Repealed}

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} A reference in these Regulations to an incorporated
  classification, standard, procedure or other specification shall be
  interpreted as excluding the following phrases that appear in that
  material:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} "acceptable to the Administration";

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} "to the satisfaction of the Administration";

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} "in the opinion of the Administration";

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} "acceptable to the approval authority";

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} "approved by the approval authority"; and

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} "accepted by the commandant".

## [Application]{.HTitleText1} {#h-517305 .Part}

### [Where Regulations Do Not Apply]{.HTitleText2} {#h-517306 .Subheading}

**[[3]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-3 .sectionLabel}** These Regulations do not
apply in respect of

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} fishing vessels;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} pleasure craft;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} ships that are five tons gross tonnage or under and
  are certified to carry 12 passengers or fewer;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} cargo ships that are 15 tons gross tonnage or under;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} ships capable of engaging in the drilling for, or the
  production, conservation or processing of, oil or gas; or

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} ships to which the [Vessel Construction and Equipment
  Regulations](/eng/regulations/SOR-2023-257) apply.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/80-685, s. 2
- SOR/96-218, s. 1
- SOR/2001-179, s. 2
- [SOR/2023-257, s. 431](#1431226-1435601){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1431226-1435601.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-3-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 3"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Where Regulations Apply]{.HTitleText2} {#h-517315 .Subheading}

- **[[4]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-4 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Parts I
  and III of these Regulations apply in respect of existing ships.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Parts II and III of these Regulations apply in
  respect of new ships.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-218, s. 1
:::

## [General]{.HTitleText1} {#h-517321 .Part}

### [Classes of Ships]{.HTitleText2} {#h-517322 .Subheading}

**[[5]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-5 .sectionLabel}** For the purposes of these
Regulations, ships are divided into the following classes:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} Class I consists of ships that are over five tons
  gross tonnage and are

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} Safety Convention ships that are certified to carry
    more than 12 passengers on unlimited voyages, or

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} ships that are not Safety Convention ships and
    that are certified to carry more than 12 passengers on unlimited
    voyages or near coastal voyages, Class 1;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} Class II consists of ships that are over five tons
  gross tonnage and are

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} Safety Convention ships that are certified to carry
    more than 12 passengers on short international voyages, or

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} ships that are not Safety Convention ships and
    that are certified to carry more than 12 passengers on near coastal
    voyages, Class 1, limited to 120 nautical miles from shore;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} Class III consists of ships that are over five tons
  gross tonnage, are not Safety Convention ships and are certified to
  carry more than 12 passengers on near coastal voyages, Class 2, or
  inland voyages;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} Class IV consists of ships that are over five tons
  gross tonnage, are not Safety Convention ships and are certified to
  carry more than 12 passengers on inland voyages, limited to 25
  nautical miles from shore, or near coastal voyages, Class 2, limited
  to five nautical miles from shore;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} Class V consists of ships that are over five tons
  gross tonnage, are not Safety Convention ships and are certified to
  carry more than 12 passengers on sheltered waters voyages;

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} Class VI consists of ships that are not over five
  tons gross tonnage and that are certified to carry more than 12
  passengers;

- [(g)]{.lawlabel} Class VII consists of ships that are over five tons
  gross tonnage, are certified to carry passengers, are not
  self-propelled and are towed or pushed by a ship or operated by a
  cable;

- [(h)]{.lawlabel} Class VIII \[*reserved*\];

- [(i)]{.lawlabel} Class IX consists of ships that are over 15 tons
  gross tonnage and are

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} Safety Convention ships that are not certified to
    carry passengers, or that are certified to carry 12 or fewer
    passengers, on unlimited voyages, or

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} ships that are not Safety Convention ships and
    that are not certified to carry passengers, or that are certified to
    carry 12 or fewer passengers, on unlimited voyages or near coastal
    voyages, Class 1;

- [(j)]{.lawlabel} Class X consists of ships that are over 15 tons gross
  tonnage, are not Safety Convention ships and are not certified to
  carry passengers, or are certified to carry 12 or fewer passengers, on
  near coastal voyages, Class 1, limited to 120 nautical miles from
  shore, near coastal voyages, Class 2, sheltered waters voyages, inland
  voyages, inland voyages, limited to 25 nautical miles from shore, or
  near coastal voyages, Class 2, limited to five nautical miles from
  shore; and

- [(k)]{.lawlabel} Class XI consists of ships that are over 15 tons
  gross tonnage, are not certified to carry passengers but carry a crew,
  are not self-propelled and are towed or pushed by a ship or operated
  by a cable.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/80-685, s. 3
- SOR/96-218, s. 1
- [SOR/2023-257, s. 432](#1431228-1435605){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1431228-1435605.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-5-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 5"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Additional Equipment]{.HTitleText2} {#h-517343 .Subheading}

**[[5.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-5.1 .sectionLabel}** A ship may carry life
saving equipment that is in addition to that required by these
Regulations, if the additional equipment

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} does not present a safety hazard;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} is not detrimental to the seaworthiness of the ship;
  and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} meets the requirements of these Regulations.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-218, s. 1
:::

### [Marine Evacuation Systems]{.HTitleText2} {#h-517350 .Subheading}

**[[5.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-5.2 .sectionLabel}** If a ship is required
to carry life rafts under Part I or II, a marine evacuation system may
be substituted for the life rafts and any associated launching devices
if

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the accommodation capacity of the life rafts of the
  marine evacuation system is at least equal to the accommodation
  capacity of the life rafts for which the marine evacuation system is
  substituted; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the marine evacuation system meets the requirements
  of paragraph 6.2 of Chapter VI of the LSA Code and is approved as
  having met those requirements.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-218, s. 1
- SOR/2001-179, s. 3
- [SOR/2023-257, s. 433](#1431230-1435625){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1431230-1435625.html"
  role="button"}
:::

[Previous
Version](section-5.2-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 5.2"){.PITLink}
:::

## [PART I]{.HLabel1}[Equipment to Be Carried by Existing Ships]{.HTitleText1} {#h-517356 .Part}

### [Upgrading Provisions for Existing Ships]{.HTitleText2} {#h-517357 .Subheading}

- **[[6]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-6
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Notwithstanding any other provision
  in this Part, an existing ship may carry, instead of the equipment
  required to be carried under this Part, the equipment required to be
  carried by a new ship of its class under Part II, if that equipment
  meets the requirements set out or referred to in Part III for
  equipment carried by a new ship.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Notwithstanding sections 7 to 31, where a survival
  craft that was carried on an existing ship on April 27, 1996, and is
  set out in column I of an item of the table to this subsection is
  replaced, its replacement shall be the survival craft set out in
  column II of that item.

  <figure id="517361">
  <table
  class="table table-hover table-striped tablePointsize9 topdouble topbot">
  <thead>
  <tr class="topdouble" style="height:">
  <th class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom top"></th>
  <th class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom top" scope="col">Column I</th>
  <th class="noBorderBottom top" scope="col">Column II</th>
  </tr>
  <tr style="height:">
  <th class="noBorderRight borderBottom bottom" scope="col">Item</th>
  <th class="noBorderRight borderBottom bottom" scope="col">Survival Craft
  on Existing Ship</th>

**[[6.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-6.1 .sectionLabel}** [\[Repealed,
SOR/2001-179, s. 5\]]{.Repealed}

### [Class I Ships]{.HTitleText2}[(Ships that are over five tons gross tonnage and are (i) Safety Convention ships that are certified to carry more than 12 passengers on unlimited voyages, or (ii) ships that are not Safety Convention ships and that are certified to carry more than 12 passengers on unlimited voyages or near coastal voyages, Class 1)]{.HTitleText2} {#h-517370 .Subheading}

::: HistoricalNote
\[

- SOR/96-218, s. 2
- SOR/2023-257, s. 434

\]
:::

**[[7]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-7 .sectionLabel}** Every Class I ship making
unlimited voyages shall carry

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} on each side thereof, one or more Class 1 lifeboats,
  each at least 7.3 m in length, each under davits, and of sufficient
  aggregate capacity to accommodate 50 per cent of the complement; or

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} alternatively, in lieu of the requirements of
  paragraph (a),

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} on each side thereof, one or more Class 1
    lifeboats, each at least 7.3 m in length, each under davits, and of
    sufficient aggregate capacity to accommodate at least 37 1/2 per
    cent of the complement,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} life rafts capable of being launched by launching
    devices, of aggregate capacity sufficient to accommodate the portion
    of the complement not accommodated under subparagraph (i), and

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} launching devices sufficient in number to launch
    all the life rafts required under subparagraph (ii), fully loaded,
    in 30 minutes in calm conditions;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} on each side of the ship, a davit-launched approved
  boat that is not over 8.5 m in length and may count towards the
  requirements of paragraph (a) or (b) if it is a Class 1 lifeboat;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} on each side, under davits, one Class 1 motor
  lifeboat at least 7.3 m in length, that may count towards the
  requirements of paragraph (a) or (b), but a ship whose complement is
  under 31 may carry a single motor lifeboat;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} enough life rafts capable of being launched by
  launching devices for 25 per cent of the complement;

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} where the ship is within the length range set out in
  column I of an item of the table to this paragraph, the supply of
  equipment set out in columns II to V of that item:

  <figure id="517383">
  <table
  class="table table-hover table-striped tablePointsize8 topdouble topbot">

- [(g)]{.lawlabel} the following supply of lifejackets, each fitted with
  a whistle and a personal locator light:

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} one for each member of the complement,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} enough conspicuously stowed on deck for 5 per cent
    of the complement, and

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} enough that are suitable for children for at
    least 10 per cent of the complement or one for each child on board,
    whichever is greater;

- [(h)]{.lawlabel} three survival craft VHF radiotelephone apparatus
  stowed so that they are readily accessible for immediate use;

- [(i)]{.lawlabel} for each life raft, a SOLAS A pack as set out in the
  LSA Code;

- [(j)]{.lawlabel} for each approved boat, the equipment set out in
  section 2 of Schedule II;

- [(k)]{.lawlabel} a line-throwing appliance;

- [(l)]{.lawlabel} 12 rocket parachute flares;

- [(m)]{.lawlabel} two SARTs stowed so that they are readily accessible
  for immediate use and for placing in the two survival craft that are
  launched first, if the ship is

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a Safety Convention ship, or

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} 20 m or over in length; and

- [(n)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2000-261, s. 2\]]{.Repealed}

- [(o)]{.lawlabel} means of embarkation into survival craft.

- [(p) and (q)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/96-218, s. 3\]]{.Repealed}

**[[8]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-8 .sectionLabel}** Every Class I ship making
unlimited voyages and that is not a Safety Convention ship shall comply
with the requirements of section 7.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2023-257, s. 436](#1431241-1435649){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1431241-1435649.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-8-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 8"){.PITLink}
:::

**[[9]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-9 .sectionLabel}** Every Class I ship making
near coastal voyages, Class 1 and that is not a Safety Convention ship
shall comply with the requirements of section 7 except that

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} not more than one motor lifeboat need be carried;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/96-218, s. 4\]]{.Repealed}

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} in lieu of the quantity requirements of paragraph
  7(e) life rafts of aggregate capacity sufficient to accommodate 10 per
  cent of the complement shall be carried;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} in lieu of the requirements of subparagraphs 7(g)(ii)
  and (iii), additional lifejackets, equal in number to 50 per cent of
  the number of berthed passengers, each lifejacket fitted with a
  whistle and a personal locator light, shall be carried conspicuously
  stowed on deck.

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/96-218, s. 4\]]{.Repealed}

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-218, s. 4
- [SOR/2023-257, s. 437](#1431243-1435651){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1431243-1435651.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-9-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 9"){.PITLink}
:::

**[[9.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-9.1 .sectionLabel}** A Class I ship shall be
provided with signs that indicate

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the location of

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} survival craft and their launching devices,

  - [(i.1)]{.lawlabel} lifejackets and lifejackets suitable for
    children,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} muster stations, and

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} embarkation stations; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} directions to the muster and embarkation stations.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-218, s. 5
- SOR/2004-26, s. 2
:::

### [Class II Ships]{.HTitleText2}[(Ships that are over five tons gross tonnage and are (i) Safety Convention ships that are certified to carry more than 12 passengers on short international voyages, or (ii) ships that are not Safety Convention ships and that are certified to carry more than 12 passengers on near coastal voyages, Class 1, limited to 120 nautical miles from shore)]{.HTitleText2} {#h-517426 .Subheading}

::: HistoricalNote
\[

- SOR/96-218, s. 6
- SOR/2023-257, s. 499

\]
:::

**[[10]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-10 .sectionLabel}** Every Class II ship
making short international voyages more than 20 miles from land shall
carry

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} subject to subparagraphs (i) and (ii) and paragraph
  (g), Class 1 lifeboats, each at least 7.3 m in length, each under
  davits, in number and capacity in accordance with columns I and III of
  the following table:

  except that

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} where the Board considers the requirements of
    Column I to be unreasonable or impracticable, compliance with the
    requirements of Column II may be permitted in lieu thereof, and

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the capacity of the lifeboats need not be greater
    than is necessary to accommodate the complement;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} on each side of the ship, a davit-launched approved
  boat that is not over 8.5 m in length and may count towards the
  requirements of paragraph (a) or (g) if it is a Class 1 lifeboat;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} on each side, one Class 1 motor lifeboat at least 7.3
  m in length, each under davits, that may count towards the
  requirements of paragraph (a) or (g), except that a ship whose
  complement is under 31 may carry a single motor lifeboat;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} where the lifeboats referred to in paragraph (a) are
  insufficient to accommodate the complement, additional survival craft
  to make up the deficiency, consisting of one of the following or a
  combination of the two:

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} Class 1 lifeboats, each at least 7.3 m in length
    and under davits, or

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} where the special subdivision requirements of the
    [Hull Construction Regulations](/eng/regulations/C.R.C.,_c._1431)
    are complied with, life rafts or, if the embarkation station is 4.57
    m or more above the waterline when the ship is in its lightest
    seagoing condition, life rafts under launching devices that are
    capable of launching the life rafts within 30 minutes in calm
    conditions;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} life rafts of aggregate capacity equal to 10 per cent
  of the lifeboat capacity, capable of being launched by launching
  devices where such devices are fitted under paragraph (d);

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/96-218, s. 7\]]{.Repealed}

- [(g)]{.lawlabel} notwithstanding the quantity requirements of
  paragraph (a), where the stowage of life rafts in accordance with
  subparagraph (d)(ii) is impracticable because of insufficient deck
  space, fewer lifeboats, except that

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a ship 57.9 m in length or over shall carry on each
    side, under davits, at least 2 Class 1 lifeboats at least 7.3 m in
    length, and

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} a ship under 57.9 m in length shall carry on each
    side, under davits, at least 1 Class 1 lifeboat at least 7.3 m in
    length,

  and such lifeboats shall, together with additional equipment as
  described under paragraph (d), accommodate the complement;

- [(h)]{.lawlabel} where the ship is within the length range set out in
  column I of an item of the table to this paragraph, the supply of
  equipment set out in columns II to V of that item:

- [(i)]{.lawlabel} the following supply of lifejackets:

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} one for each member of the complement,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} enough conspicuously stowed on deck for 5 per cent
    of the complement, and

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} enough that are suitable for children for at
    least 10 per cent of the complement or one for each child on board,
    whichever is greater;

- [(j)]{.lawlabel} three survival craft VHF radiotelephone apparatus
  stowed so that they are readily accessible for immediate use;

- [(k)]{.lawlabel} for each life raft,

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} if the ship is engaged on a near coastal voyage,
    Class 1, limited to 120 nautical miles from shore, a SOLAS A pack as
    set out in the LSA Code, and

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} if the ship is engaged on a short international
    voyage that is not a sheltered waters voyage, a SOLAS B pack as set
    out in the LSA Code;

- [(l)]{.lawlabel} for each approved boat, the equipment set out in
  section 2 of Schedule II;

- [(m)]{.lawlabel} a line-throwing appliance;

- [(n)]{.lawlabel} 12 rocket parachute flares;

- [(o)]{.lawlabel} two SARTs stowed so that they are readily accessible
  for immediate use and for placing in the two survival craft that are
  launched first, if the ship is

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a Safety Convention ship, or

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} 20 m or over in length and engaged on a voyage
    beyond the VHF coverage area or beyond sea area A1; and

- [(p)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2000-261, s. 3\]]{.Repealed}

- [(q)]{.lawlabel} means of embarkation into survival craft.

**[[11]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-11 .sectionLabel}** Every Class II ship
making short international voyages not more than 20 miles from land,
other than sheltered waters voyages, shall carry

  except that

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} where the Board considers the requirements of
    Column I to be unreasonable or impracticable, compliance with the
    requirements of Column II may be permitted in lieu thereof, and

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the necessity of the lifeboats need not be greater
    than is necessary to accommodate the complement;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} if the ship is 30.5 m or over in length, on each side
  of the ship, an approved boat that is not over 8.5 m in length and may
  count towards the requirements of paragraph (a) or (f) if it is a
  Class 1 lifeboat;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} where the lifeboats referred to in paragraph (a) are
  insufficient to accommodate the complement, additional survival craft
  to make up the deficiency, consisting of one of the following or a
  combination of the two:

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} Class 1 lifeboats, each at least 7.3 m in length
    and under davits, or

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} where the special subdivision requirements of the
    [Vessel Construction and Equipment
    Regulations](/eng/regulations/SOR-2023-257) or the [Hull
    Construction Regulations](/eng/regulations/C.R.C.,_c._1431), as
    applicable, are complied with, life rafts or, if the embarkation
    station is 4.57 m or more above the waterline when the ship is in
    its lightest seagoing condition, life rafts under launching devices
    that are capable of launching the life rafts within 30 minutes in
    calm conditions;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} life rafts of aggregate capacity equal to 10 per cent
  of the lifeboat capacity, capable of being launched by launching
  devices where such devices are fitted under paragraph (c);

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/96-218, s. 8\]]{.Repealed}

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} notwithstanding the quantity requirements of
  paragraph (a), where the stowage of life rafts as set out in
  subparagraph (c)(ii) is impracticable because of insufficient deck
  space, fewer lifeboats, except that

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a ship 57.9 m in length or over shall carry on each
    side, under davits, at least 2 Class 1 lifeboats at least 7.3 m in
    length, and

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} a ship under 57.9 m in length shall carry on each
    side, under davits, at least 1 Class 1 lifeboat at least 7.3 m in
    length,

  and that such lifeboats shall, together with additional equipment as
  described under paragraph (c), accommodate the complement;

- [(g)]{.lawlabel} where the ship is within the length range set out in
  column I of an item of the table to this paragraph, the supply of
  equipment set out in columns II to V of that item:

**[[12]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-12 .sectionLabel}** Every Class II ship
making short international voyages that are sheltered waters voyages,
shall carry

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} subject to subparagraphs (i) and (ii) and to
  paragraph (d), Class 1 or Class 2 lifeboats each at least 7.3 m in
  length, each under davits, in number and capacity in accordance with
  columns I and III of the following table:

  except that

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} where the Board considers the requirements of
    Column I to be unreasonable or impracticable, compliance with the
    requirements of Column II may be permitted in lieu thereof, and

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the capacity of the lifeboats need not be greater
    than is necessary to accommodate the complement;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} where the lifeboats referred to in paragraph (a) are
  insufficient to accommodate the complement, additional survival craft
  to make up the deficiency, consisting of one of the following or a
  combination of the two:

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} Class 1 or Class 2 lifeboats, each at least 7.3 m
    in length and under davits, or

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} where the special subdivision requirements of the
    [Vessel Construction and Equipment
    Regulations](/eng/regulations/SOR-2023-257) or the [Hull
    Construction Regulations](/eng/regulations/C.R.C.,_c._1431), as
    applicable, are complied with, life rafts or, if the embarkation
    station is 4.57 m or more above the waterline when the ship is in
    its lightest seagoing condition, life rafts under launching devices
    that are capable of launching the life rafts within 30 minutes in
    calm conditions;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/96-218, s. 9\]]{.Repealed}

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} notwithstanding the quantity requirements of
  paragraph (a), where the stowage of life rafts in accordance with
  subparagraph (b)(ii) is impracticable because of insufficient deck
  space, fewer lifeboats, except that

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a ship 57.9 m in length or over shall carry on each
    side, under davits, at least 2 Class 1 or Class 2 lifeboats at least
    7.3 m in length, and

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} a ship under 57.9 m in length shall carry on each
    side, under davits, at least 1 Class 1 or Class 2 lifeboat at least
    7.3 m in length,

  and such lifeboats shall, together with additional equipment as
  described under paragraph (b), accommodate the complement;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} where the ship is within the length range set out in
  column I of an item of the table to this paragraph, the supply of
  equipment set out in columns II and III of that item:

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} the following supply of lifejackets:

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} one for each member of the complement,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} enough conspicuously stowed on deck for 5 per cent
    of the complement, and

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} enough that are suitable for children for at
    least 10 per cent of the complement or one for each child on board,
    whichever is greater;

- [(g)]{.lawlabel} three survival craft VHF radiotelephone apparatus
  stowed so that they are readily accessible for immediate use;

- [(h)]{.lawlabel} for each life raft, a SOLAS B pack as set out in the
  LSA Code;

- [(i)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2023-257, s. 440\]]{.Repealed}

- [(j)]{.lawlabel} means of embarkation into survival craft; and

- [(k)]{.lawlabel} six red hand flares.

**[[13]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-13 .sectionLabel}** Every Class II ship
making near coastal voyages, Class 1, limited to 120 nautical miles from
shore other than a Safety Convention ship, shall comply with the
requirements of section 10, except that

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} where accommodation for less than 300 persons is
  provided in life rafts, only one motor lifeboat need be carried, but
  where accommodation for 300 persons or more is provided in life rafts,
  a second motor lifeboat or mechanically propelled lifeboat shall be
  carried; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} if the ship is less than 20 m in length and engaged
  on a voyage that does not go beyond the Gulf of St. Lawrence, it is
  not required to carry the survival craft VHF radiotelephone apparatus
  referred to in paragraph 10(j).

- [(c) to (e)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/96-218, s. 10\]]{.Repealed}

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-218, s. 10
- SOR/2000-261, s. 6
- [SOR/2023-257, s. 499](#1431420-1436004){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1431420-1436004.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-13-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 13"){.PITLink}
:::

**[[13.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-13.1 .sectionLabel}** A Class II ship shall
be provided with signs that indicate

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the location of

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} survival craft and their launching devices,

  - [(i.1)]{.lawlabel} lifejackets and lifejackets suitable for
    children,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} muster stations, and

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} embarkation stations; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} directions to the muster and embarkation stations.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-218, s. 11
- SOR/2004-26, s. 6
:::

### [Class III Ships]{.HTitleText2}[(Ships that are over five tons gross tonnage, are not Safety Convention ships and are certified to carry more than 12 passengers on near coastal voyages, Class 2, or inland voyages)]{.HTitleText2} {#h-517573 .Subheading}

::: HistoricalNote
\[

- SOR/2023-257, s. 501

\]
:::

**[[14]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-14 .sectionLabel}** A Class III ship shall
carry

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} where the ship is 18.3 m or under in length, the
  following survival craft:

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} one Class 1 lifeboat of at least 1.59 m^3^ capacity
    with a means of launching, and

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} one or more life rafts of an aggregate capacity
    sufficient to accommodate the complement;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} where the ship is over 18.3 m but under 22.9 m in
  length, one Class 1 lifeboat that is under davits and is of at least
  2.12 m^3^ capacity;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} where the ship is 22.9 m or over in length, on each
  side of the ship, one Class 1 lifeboat that is under davits and has
  the appropriate capacity for the length of the ship, in accordance
  with the table to this paragraph, except that the lifeboats need not
  be of a greater aggregate capacity than is sufficient to accommodate
  the complement:

  </tr>

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} where the lifeboats referred to in paragraph (b)
  or (c) are insufficient to accommodate the complement, additional
  survival craft to make up the deficiency, consisting of one of the
  following or a combination of the two:

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} equally distributed on both sides of the ship,
    Class 1 lifeboats, each under davits and of the appropriate capacity
    for the length of the ship, in accordance with the table to
    paragraph (c), or

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} life rafts or, if the embarkation station is 4.57
    m or more above the waterline when the ship is in its lightest
    seagoing condition and the life rafts that are embarked at that
    station accommodate in total more than 50 persons, life rafts under
    launching devices that are capable of launching the life rafts
    within 30 minutes in calm conditions;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} where life rafts are carried pursuant to subparagraph
  (d)(ii), additional life rafts that

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} have an aggregate capacity equal to 10 per cent of
    the capacity of the lifeboats carried, and

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} where the life rafts that are carried pursuant to
    subparagraph (d)(ii) are under launching devices, are capable of
    being launched by the launching devices;

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} where the ship is within the length range set out in
  column I of an item of a table to this paragraph, the supply of
  equipment set out in columns II to IV of

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} where the ship is engaged on a near coastal voyage,
    Class 2, that item in Table I, and

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} where the ship is engaged on an inland voyage,
    that item in Table II:

    <figure id="517595">
    <table
    class="table table-hover table-striped tablePointsize9 topdouble topbot">

    </tr>
    <tr>
    <th class="noBorderRight borderBottom bottom" scope="col">Item</th>
    <th class="noBorderRight borderBottom bottom" scope="col">Length of
    Ship</th>
    <th class="noBorderRight borderBottom bottom" scope="col">Lifebuoys</th>
    <th class="noBorderRight borderBottom bottom" scope="col">Self-igniting
    Lights</th>
    <th class="borderBottom bottom" scope="col">Buoyant Lifelines</th>

- [(g)]{.lawlabel} the following supply of lifejackets:

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} one for each member of the complement,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} enough conspicuously stowed on deck for 5 per cent
    of the complement, and

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} enough that are suitable for children for at
    least 10 per cent of the complement or one for each child on board,
    whichever is greater;

- [(h)]{.lawlabel} for each life raft, a SOLAS B pack as set out in the
  LSA Code;

- [(i)]{.lawlabel} for each approved boat, the equipment set out in
  section 2 of Schedule II;

- [(j)]{.lawlabel} where the ship is over 45.7 m in length, a
  line-throwing appliance;

- [(k)]{.lawlabel} 12 pyrotechnic distress signals of which six are
  rocket parachute flares and six are rocket parachute flares or red
  hand flares;

- [(l)]{.lawlabel} means of embarkation into survival craft; and

- [(m)]{.lawlabel} if the ship is 20 m or over in length and engaged on
  a near coastal voyage, Class 2, three survival craft VHF
  radiotelephone apparatus stowed so that they are readily accessible
  for immediate use.

**[[15]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-15 .sectionLabel}** A Class III ship shall be
provided with signs that indicate

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the location of

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} survival craft and their launching devices,

  - [(i.1)]{.lawlabel} lifejackets and lifejackets suitable for
    children,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} muster stations, and

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} embarkation stations; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} directions to the muster and embarkation stations.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/80-685, s. 9
- SOR/96-218, s. 12
- SOR/2004-26, s. 8
:::

**[[15.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-15.1 .sectionLabel}** Instead of complying
with sections 14 and 15, a ship may comply with section 16 if it

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} was a Class IV ship and an existing ship on the day
  before the day on which subsection 148(4) comes into force;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} engages only on inland voyages, Class II, or minor
  waters voyages, Class I, or both, as those voyages are defined in
  subsections 5(2) and 6(1) of the [Home-Trade, Inland and Minor Waters
  Voyages Regulations](/eng/regulations/C.R.C.,_c._1430); and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} does not change its area of operation or equipment.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2023-257, s. 442](#1431273-1435680){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1431273-1435680.html"
  role="button"}
:::

### [Class IV Ships]{.HTitleText2}[(Ships that are over five tons gross tonnage, are not Safety Convention ships and are certified to carry more than 12 passengers on inland voyages, limited to 25 nautical miles from shore, or near coastal voyages, Class 2, limited to five nautical miles from shore)]{.HTitleText2} {#h-517635 .Subheading}

::: HistoricalNote
\[

- SOR/2023-257, s. 504

\]
:::

- **[[16]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-16 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  Class IV ship shall carry

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} subject to paragraph (b), at least one Class 1
    lifeboat under davits and of the appropriate capacity for the length
    of the ship in accordance with the table to this paragraph, except
    that the lifeboats need not be of a greater aggregate capacity than
    is sufficient to accommodate the complement:

    <figure id="517639">

    Lifeboat (m<sup>3</sup>)</th>
    </tr>
    </thead>

    <td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom top">1</td>
    <td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom top">22.9 m or over but under
    30.5 m</td>
    <td class="noBorderBottom top">3.540</td>
    </tr>
    <tr>
    <td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom top">2</td>
    <td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom top">30.5 m or over but under
    45.7 m</td>

    </tr>

    <td class="top">8.495</td>
    </tr>

    </figure>

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if the carriage of a lifeboat referred to in
    paragraph (a) is impracticable, an approved boat;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} enough life rafts to accommodate that part of the
    complement not accommodated in the lifeboat or emergency boat;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} where the embarkation station for life rafts is
    4.57 m or more above the waterline when the ship is in its lightest
    seagoing condition and the life rafts that are embarked at that
    station accommodate in total more than 50 persons, launching devices
    that are capable of launching the life rafts within 30 minutes in
    calm conditions;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} where the ship is within the length range set out
    in column I of an item of the table to this paragraph, the supply of
    equipment set out in columns II to IV of that item:

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} the following supply of lifejackets:

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} one for each member of the complement, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} enough that are suitable for children for at
      least 10 per cent of the complement or one for each child on
      board, whichever is greater;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} for each life raft, a SOLAS B pack as set out in
    the LSA Code;

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} for each approved boat, the equipment set out in
    section 2 of Schedule II;

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} 12 pyrotechnic distress signals of which six are
    rocket parachute flares; and

  - [(j)]{.lawlabel} means of embarkation into survival craft.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} A Class IV ship shall be provided with signs that
  indicate

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the location of

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} survival craft and their launching devices,

    - [(i.1)]{.lawlabel} lifejackets and lifejackets suitable for
      children,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} muster stations, and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} embarkation stations; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} directions to the muster and embarkation stations.

**[[16.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-16.1 .sectionLabel}** Instead of complying
with section 16, a ship may comply with section 17 if it

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} was a Class V ship and an existing ship on the day
  before the day on which subsection 148(4) comes into force;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} engages only on home-trade voyages, Class IV, or
  minor waters voyages, Class II, or both, as those voyages are defined
  in subsections 4(4) and 6(2) of the [Home-Trade, Inland and Minor
  Waters Voyages Regulations](/eng/regulations/C.R.C.,_c._1430); and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} does not change its area of operation or equipment.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2023-257, s. 444](#1431277-1435692){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1431277-1435692.html"
  role="button"}
:::

### [Class V Ships]{.HTitleText2}[(Ships that are over five tons gross tonnage, are not Safety Convention ships and are certified to carry more than 12 passengers on sheltered waters voyages)]{.HTitleText2} {#h-517675 .Subheading}

::: HistoricalNote
\[

- SOR/96-218, s. 13
- SOR/2023-257, s. 500

\]
:::

- **[[17]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-17 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  Class V ship shall carry

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} if such ship is 45.7 m or over in length, on each
    side thereof one or more Class 2 lifeboats, each at least 4.9 m in
    length and each under davits, except that where carriage of
    lifeboats on each side is unreasonable or impracticable, they may be
    carried on one side only;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if such ship is 45.7 m but not more than 22.9 m in
    length, one or more Class 2 lifeboats, each of at least 2.12 m^3^
    capacity and each under davits;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} if the ship is 22.9 m or under in length, where
    practicable, a suitable boat; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} where the capacity of the survival craft referred
    to in paragraph (a), (b) or (c) is insufficient to accommodate the
    complement, additional Class 2 lifeboats that have the appropriate
    capacities or life rafts or inflatable rescue platforms, to make up
    the deficiency.

  - [(e) to (h)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/96-218, s. 14\]]{.Repealed}

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} If a ship navigates in waters the temperature of
  which is 15°C or more, the requirement in respect of the accommodation
  capacity of the life rafts or inflatable rescue platforms that is
  referred to in paragraph (1)(d) or (3)(b) may be met by counting not
  more than 33.33 per cent of the complement of the life raft or
  inflatable rescue platform as being in the water, holding on to the
  life raft or inflatable rescue platform.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} A Class V ship that navigates in waters the
  temperature of which is 15°C or more may carry,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} instead of the survival craft referred to in
    subsection (1), one lifebuoy for every four members of the
    complement if the ship navigates

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} within 150 m of shore, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} in a depth of water not exceeding 1.5 m; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} instead of the survival craft referred to in
    paragraph (1)(d), enough buoyant apparatus to accommodate not more
    than 40 per cent of the complement of the ship and enough Class 2
    lifeboats, life rafts or inflatable rescue platforms to accommodate
    that portion of the complement not accommodated by the buoyant
    apparatus.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2006-256, s. 1\]]{.Repealed}

- [(5)]{.lawlabel} A Class V ship shall carry

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the following lifebuoys:

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} where the ship is under 45.7 m in length, at
      least four lifebuoys, two of which each have a self-igniting light
      attached and another two of which each have a buoyant lifeline
      attached, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} where the ship is 45.7 m or over in length, at
      least six lifebuoys, two of which each have a self-igniting light
      attached and two others of which each have a buoyant lifeline
      attached;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the following supply of lifejackets:

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} one for each member of the complement, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} enough that are suitable for children for at
      least 10 per cent of the complement or one for each child on
      board, whichever is greater;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} for each life raft, a SOLAS B pack as set out in
    the LSA Code;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2023-257, s. 451\]]{.Repealed}

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} for each suitable boat, the equipment set out in
    section 5 of Schedule II; and

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} six pyrotechnic distress signals of which three are
    rocket parachute flares.

- [(6)]{.lawlabel} A Class V ship shall be provided with signs that
  indicate

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the location of

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} survival craft and their launching devices,

    - [(i.1)]{.lawlabel} lifejackets and lifejackets suitable for
      children,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} muster stations, and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} embarkation stations; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} directions to the muster and embarkation stations.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/80-685, s. 11
- SOR/85-859, s. 1
- SOR/96-218, s. 14
- SOR/2001-179, s. 12
- SOR/2004-26, s. 10
- SOR/2006-256, s. 1
- SOR/2013-235, s. 1

  role="button"}
:::

### [Class VI Ships]{.HTitleText2}[(Ships that are not over five tons gross tonnage and that are certified to carry more than 12 passengers)]{.HTitleText2} {#h-517712 .Subheading}

- **[[18]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-18 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  Class VI ship shall carry

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} where the ship is engaged on a sheltered waters
    voyage, enough life rafts or inflatable rescue platforms to
    accommodate the complement; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} where the ship is engaged on any other voyage,
    enough life rafts to accommodate the complement.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} If a ship navigates in waters the temperature of
  which is 15°C or more, the requirement in respect of the accommodation
  capacity of the life rafts or inflatable rescue platforms that is
  referred to in paragraph (1)(a) or (3)(b) may be met by counting not
  more than 33.33 per cent of the complement of the life raft or
  inflatable rescue platform as being in the water, holding on to the
  life raft or inflatable rescue platform.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} A Class VI ship that navigates in waters the
  temperature of which is 15°C or more may carry, instead of the
  survival craft referred to in paragraph (1)(a),

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} one lifebuoy for every four members of the
    complement if the ship navigates

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} within 150 m of shore, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} in a depth of water not exceeding 1.5 m; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} enough buoyant apparatus to accommodate not more
    than 40 per cent of the complement of the ship and enough life rafts
    or inflatable rescue platforms to accommodate that portion of the
    complement not accommodated by the buoyant apparatus.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2006-256, s. 2\]]{.Repealed}

- [(5)]{.lawlabel} A Class VI ship shall carry the following supply of
  lifejackets:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} one for each member of the complement; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} enough that are suitable for children for at least
    10 per cent of the complement or one for each child on board,
    whichever is greater.

- [(6)]{.lawlabel} A Class VI ship shall carry the following equipment:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} for each life raft or inflatable rescue platform,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} if the ship is engaged on a voyage beyond the
      limits of a near coastal voyage, Class 2, a SOLAS A pack as set
      out in the LSA Code, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} if the ship is engaged on any other voyage, a
      SOLAS B pack as set out in the LSA Code;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} except if the ship is engaged on a sheltered waters
    voyage, six pyrotechnic distress signals of which three are rocket
    parachute flares; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} two lifebuoys, one of which has a buoyant lifeline
    attached.

- [(7)]{.lawlabel} A Class VI ship shall be provided with signs that
  indicate the location of life saving equipment that is not stowed in
  plain view.

::: HistoricalNote

:::

### [Class VII Ships]{.HTitleText2}[(Ships that are over five tons gross tonnage, are certified to carry passengers, are not self-propelled and are towed or pushed by a ship or operated by a cable)]{.HTitleText2} {#h-517739 .Subheading}

- **[[19]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-19 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  Class VII ship shall carry

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} where the ship is engaged on a sheltered waters
    voyage, enough life rafts or inflatable rescue platforms to
    accommodate the complement; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} where the ship is engaged on any other voyage,
    enough life rafts to accommodate the complement.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} If a ship navigates in waters the temperature of
  which is 15°C or more, the requirement in respect of the accommodation
  capacity of the life rafts or inflatable rescue platforms that is
  referred to in paragraph (1)(a) or (3)(b) may be met by counting not
  more than 33.33 per cent of the complement of the life raft or
  inflatable rescue platform as being in the water, holding on to the
  life raft or inflatable rescue platform.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} A Class VII ship that navigates in waters the
  temperature of which is 15°C or more may carry, instead of the
  survival craft referred to in paragraph (1)(a),

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} one lifebuoy for every four members of the
    complement if the ship navigates

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} within 150 m of shore, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} in a depth of water not exceeding 1.5 m; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} enough buoyant apparatus to accommodate not more
    than 40 per cent of the complement of the ship and enough life rafts
    or inflatable rescue platforms to accommodate that portion of the
    complement not accommodated by the buoyant apparatus.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2006-256, s. 3\]]{.Repealed}

- [(5)]{.lawlabel} A Class VII ship shall carry a suitable boat that is
  carried on board or towed.

- [(6)]{.lawlabel} A Class VII ship within the length range set out in
  column I of an item of the table to this subsection shall carry the
  supply of equipment set out in columns II and III of that item.

  <figure id="517753">

  class="table table-hover table-striped tablePointsize9 topdouble topbot">

- [(7)]{.lawlabel} A Class VII ship shall carry the following supply of
  lifejackets:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} one for each member of the complement; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} enough that are suitable for children for at least
    10 per cent of the complement or one for each child on board,
    whichever is greater.

- [(8)]{.lawlabel} A Class VII ship shall carry, for each life raft and
  each inflatable rescue platform, the following equipment:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} if the ship is engaged on a voyage beyond the
    limits of a near coastal voyage, Class 2, a SOLAS A pack as set out
    in the LSA Code; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if the ship is engaged on any other voyage, a SOLAS
    B pack as set out in the LSA Code.

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2023-257, s. 447\]]{.Repealed}

- [(9)]{.lawlabel} A Class VII ship shall carry, for each suitable boat,
  the equipment set out in section 5 of Schedule II.

- [(10)]{.lawlabel} A Class VII ship shall be provided with signs that
  indicate

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the location of

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} survival craft and their launching devices,

    - [(i.1)]{.lawlabel} lifejackets and lifejackets suitable for
      children,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} muster stations, and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} embarkation stations; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} directions to the muster and embarkation stations.

### [Class VIII Ships \[reserved\]]{.HTitleText2} {#h-517776 .Subheading}

::: HistoricalNote
\[

- SOR/96-218, s. 15

\]
:::

### [Class IX Ships]{.HTitleText2}[(Ships that are over 15 tons gross tonnage and are (i) Safety Convention ships that are not certified to carry passengers, or that are certified to carry 12 or fewer passengers, on unlimited voyages, or (ii) ships that are not Safety Convention ships and that are not certified to carry passengers, or that are certified to carry 12 or fewer passengers, on unlimited voyages or near coastal voyages, Class 1)]{.HTitleText2} {#h-517779 .Subheading}

::: HistoricalNote
\[

- SOR/2023-257, s. 448

\]
:::

#### [Ships Other Than Tankers]{.HTitleText3} {#h-517780 .Subheading}

::: HistoricalNote
\[

- SOR/96-218, s. 16

\]
:::

- **[[20]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-20
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), a Class
  IX ship that is not a tanker and is a Safety Convention ship or a ship
  engaged on an unlimited voyage shall carry

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} Class 1 lifeboats not less than 7.3 m in length,
    each under davits, in accordance with paragraph (b) or (c), but
    where a ship is making sheltered waters voyages, the lifeboats may
    be of Class 2;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} subject to paragraph (c),

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} on each side of the ship, one or more lifeboats
      of aggregate capacity sufficient to accommodate the complement of
      which one shall be a motor lifeboat if the ship is of 1,600 tons,
      gross tonnage, or over, but no motor lifeboat need be carried on a
      ship that does not go more than 20 miles from land,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a ship described in
      subsection (2) and a ship, other than a tug, that goes more than
      20 miles from land, one or more life rafts of aggregate capacity
      sufficient to accommodate 50 per cent of the complement, and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a tug, one or more life rafts of
      aggregate capacity sufficient to accommodate the complement;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} if such ship is a whale factory, or fish processing
    or canning factory ship,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} either

      - [(A)]{.lawlabel} lifeboats, including one motor lifeboat, of
        aggregate capacity sufficient to accommodate 50 per cent of the
        complement on each side of the ship, or

      - [(B)]{.lawlabel} lifeboats, including one motor lifeboat, of
        aggregate capacity sufficient to accommodate at least 37 1/2 per
        cent of the complement on each side of the ship and life rafts,
        capable of being launched by launching devices, of aggregate
        capacity sufficient to accommodate the portion of the complement
        not accommodated in the lifeboats and launching devices
        sufficient to launch all the life rafts required under this
        clause, fully loaded, in 30 minutes in calm conditions,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} life rafts, or additional life rafts, capable of
      being launched by launching devices if such devices are provided
      under subparagraph (i), of aggregate capacity sufficient to
      accommodate 50 per cent of the complement,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} on each side of the ship, an approved boat that
      is not over 8.5 m in length and may count towards the requirements
      of subparagraph (i) if it is a Class 1 lifeboat, and

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} notwithstanding subsection 2(1) of Part I of
      Schedule IX, gravity-type davits for the launching of lifeboats
      and for the launching of emergency boats that, pursuant to
      subparagraph (iii), are counted towards the requirements of
      subparagraph (i);

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} eight lifebuoys;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the following supply of lifejackets, each fitted
    with a whistle and a personal locator light:

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} one for each member of the complement, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} where the ship is a tug, at least two stowed in
      the wheelhouse and two stowed in the engine room;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} one immersion suit, fitted with a whistle and a
    personal locator light, for each member of the complement;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} the following survival craft VHF radiotelephone
    apparatus stowed so that they are readily accessible for immediate
    use:

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} two, if the ship is 300 tons or over but under
      500 tons gross tonnage, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} three, if the ship is 500 tons gross tonnage or
      over;

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} for each life raft,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} if the ship is engaged on a voyage beyond the
      limits of a near coastal voyage, Class 2, a SOLAS A pack as set
      out in the LSA Code, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} if the ship is engaged on any other voyage, a
      SOLAS B pack as set out in the LSA Code;

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} for each approved boat, the equipment set out in
    section 2 of Schedule II;

  - [(j)]{.lawlabel} a line-throwing appliance;

  - [(k)]{.lawlabel} if the ship is engaged on a voyage other than a
    sheltered waters voyage, 12 rocket parachute flares;

  - [(l)]{.lawlabel} the following SARTs:

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} if the ship is 20 m or over in length but under
      500 tons gross tonnage, one SART stowed so that it is readily
      accessible for immediate use and for placing in one of the
      survival craft that are launched first, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} if the ship is 500 tons gross tonnage or over,
      two SARTs stowed so that they are readily accessible for immediate
      use and for placing in the two survival craft that are launched
      first; and

  - [(m)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2000-261, s. 8\]]{.Repealed}

  - [(n)]{.lawlabel} means of embarkation into survival craft.

- [(1.1)]{.lawlabel} Despite subparagraph (1)(l)(i), a ship that is
  under 300 tons gross tonnage and that on March 31, 2001 was required
  by these Regulations to carry two Class II EPIRBs may continue to
  carry them instead of a SART until one of the batteries of the Class
  II EPIRBs needs to be replaced.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Every Class IX ship that is a bulk carrier, other
  than a tanker, that is over 91.4 m in length and makes voyages in the
  St. Lawrence River east of the Montreal entrance to the St. Lawrence
  Seaway shall include in its life saving equipment inflatable life
  rafts of sufficient capacity to accommodate half of the complement.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} Where some of the persons carried on a ship described
  in subsection (2) are berthed in the forward part of the ship,
  inflatable life rafts of sufficient capacity to accommodate all of
  those persons shall be stowed forward and the remaining inflatable
  life rafts shall be stowed aft.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} A ship of 150 m in length or over having no amidships
  superstructure shall carry, in addition to the life rafts required by
  subsection (1), a life raft that is capable of accommodating at least
  six persons and that is stowed as far forward as is reasonable and
  practicable.

- [(5)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/96-218, s. 17\]]{.Repealed}

**[[21]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-21 .sectionLabel}** [\[Repealed, SOR/96-218,
s. 18\]]{.Repealed}

- **[[22]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-22
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection 20(2), a Class
  IX ship that is engaged on a near coastal voyage, Class 1, other than
  a tanker or a Safety Convention ship, shall carry

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} if 30.5 m in length or over, either

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} on each side of such ship, one or more Class 1
      lifeboats not less than 4.9 m in length, each under davits and of
      aggregate capacity sufficient to accommodate the complement,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} on each side of the ship, one or more Class 1
      lifeboats not less than 4.9 m in length, each under davits, and
      one or more life rafts that together with the lifeboats on either
      side have sufficient capacity to accommodate the complement, the
      aggregate capacity of the life rafts and lifeboats being
      sufficient to accommodate double the complement, or

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} on one side of the ship, one or more Class 1
      lifeboats not less than 4.9 m in length, each under davits, and
      one or more life rafts that together with the lifeboats have
      sufficient capacity to accommodate double the complement, the life
      rafts alone being capable of accommodating the complement;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if such ship is under 30.5 m in length, either

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} on each side of such ship, one or more Class 1
      lifeboats not less than 4.9 m in length, each under davits and of
      aggregate capacity sufficient to accommodate the complement,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} on each side of the ship, one or more Class 1
      lifeboats not less than 4.9 m in length, each under davits, and
      one or more life rafts that together with the lifeboats on either
      side have sufficient capacity to accommodate the complement, the
      aggregate capacity of the life rafts and lifeboats being
      sufficient to accommodate 1.5 times the complement, or

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} on each side of the ship, one or more Class 1
      lifeboats not less than 4.9 m in length, each under davits, and
      one or more life rafts that together with the lifeboats have
      sufficient capacity to accommodate 1.5 times the complement, the
      life rafts alone being capable of accommodating the complement;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} if the requirements of paragraph (a) or (b) are
    impracticable, an approved boat that has a capacity of not less than
    3.54 m^3^ and carries two or more equal-sized life rafts, the
    aggregate capacity of those survival craft being sufficient to
    accommodate 200 per cent of the complement;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/96-218, s. 19\]]{.Repealed}

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} if such ship is a tug, in addition to the other
    requirements of this section, one or more life rafts with sufficient
    aggregate capacity to accommodate the complement, but any life rafts
    provided to meet the requirements of paragraph (a), (b) or (c) may
    be counted toward the requirement of this paragraph;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} if such ship is of 1,600 tons, gross tonnage, or
    over, a Class 1 motor lifeboat not less than 4.9 m in length, under
    davits, which may count towards the lifeboat requirements elsewhere
    in this section;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} where the ship is within the length range set out
    in column I of an item of the table to this paragraph, the supply of
    equipment set out in columns II to IV of that item:

    <figure id="517842">

    class="table table-hover table-striped tablePointsize9 topdouble topbot">

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} the following supply of lifejackets, each fitted
    with a whistle and a personal locator light:

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} one for each member of the complement, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} where the ship is a tug, at least two stowed in
      the wheelhouse and two stowed in the engine room;

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} one immersion suit, fitted with a whistle and a
    personal locator light, for each member of the complement;

  - [(j)]{.lawlabel} the following number of survival craft VHF
    radiotelephone apparatus stowed so that they are readily accessible
    for immediate use:

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} two, if the ship is 300 tons or over but under
      500 tons gross tonnage, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} three, if the ship is 500 tons gross tonnage or
      over;

  - [(k)]{.lawlabel} for each life raft, a SOLAS A pack as set out in
    the LSA Code;

  - [(l)]{.lawlabel} for each approved boat, the equipment set out in
    section 2 of Schedule II;

  - [(m)]{.lawlabel} where the ship is 150 tons gross tonnage or over, a
    line-throwing appliance;

  - [(n)]{.lawlabel} 12 rocket parachute flares;

  - [\[(n)]{.lawlabel} means of embarkation, in accordance with Schedule
    VI,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} at each set of davits, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} at places of embarkation into life rafts.\]

  - [(o)]{.lawlabel} the following SARTs:

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} if the ship is 20 m or over in length but under
      500 tons gross tonnage, one SART stowed so that it is readily
      accessible for immediate use and for placing in one of the
      survival craft that are launched first, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} if the ship is 500 tons gross tonnage or over,
      two SARTs stowed so that they are readily accessible for immediate
      use and for placing in the two survival craft that are launched
      first; and

  - [(p)]{.lawlabel} means of embarkation into survival craft.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Despite subparagraph (1)(o)(i), a ship that is under
  300 tons gross tonnage and that on March 31, 2001 was required by
  these Regulations to carry two Class II EPIRBs may continue to carry
  them instead of a SART until one of the batteries of the Class II
  EPIRBs needs to be replaced.
