#### [Tankers]{.HTitleText3} {#h-517871 .Subheading}

- **[[22.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-22.1 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  Class IX ship that is a tanker and is a Safety Convention ship or a
  ship engaged on an unlimited voyage shall carry

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} where the ship is under 1,600 tons gross tonnage,
    on each side of the ship, one or more Class 1 lifeboats that are
    under davits, are not under 7.3 m in length and have an aggregate
    capacity sufficient to accommodate the complement;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} where the ship is 1,600 tons or over but under
    3,000 tons gross tonnage, on each side of the ship and under
    gravity-type davits, the following lifeboats:

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} where the ship is engaged on a voyage more than
      20 nautical miles from shore, one motor lifeboat, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} one or more Class 1 lifeboats not under 7.3 m in
      length, the aggregate capacity of which is sufficient to
      accommodate that part of the complement not accommodated in the
      motor lifeboat;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} where the ship is 3,000 tons gross tonnage or over,
    four lifeboats that are under gravity-type davits and are
    distributed equally on both sides of the ship, two of which are
    carried aft and two amidships or, where the ship has no amidships
    superstructure, are carried aft;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} where the ship is engaged on a voyage more than 20
    nautical miles from shore, the following life rafts:

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} one or more life rafts having an aggregate
      capacity sufficient to accommodate 50 per cent of the complement,
      and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} where the ship is 150 m or over in length and
      has no amidships superstructure, a life raft that is capable of
      accommodating at least six persons and is stowed as far forward as
      is practicable;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} eight lifebuoys;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} one lifejacket, fitted with a whistle and a
    personal locator light, for each member of the complement;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} one immersion suit, fitted with a whistle and a
    personal locator light, for each member of the complement;

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} the following survival craft VHF radiotelephone
    apparatus stowed so that they are readily accessible for immediate
    use:

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} two, if the ship is 300 tons or over but under
      500 tons gross tonnage, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} three, if the ship is 500 tons gross tonnage or
      over;

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} for each life raft,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} if the ship is engaged on a voyage beyond the
      limits of a near coastal voyage, Class 2, a SOLAS A pack as set
      out in the LSA Code, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} if the ship is engaged on any other voyage, a
      SOLAS B pack as set out in the LSA Code;

  - [(j)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2023-257, s. 451\]]{.Repealed}

  - [(k)]{.lawlabel} a line-throwing appliance;

  - [(l)]{.lawlabel} if the ship is engaged on a voyage other than a
    sheltered waters voyage, 12 rocket parachute flares;

  - [(m)]{.lawlabel} the following SARTs:

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} if the ship is 20 m or over in length but under
      500 tons gross tonnage, one SART stowed so that it is readily
      accessible for immediate use and for placing in one of the
      survival craft that are launched first, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} if the ship is 500 tons gross tonnage or over,
      two SARTs stowed so that they are readily accessible for immediate
      use and for placing in the two survival craft that are launched
      first; and

  - [(n)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2000-261, s. 10\]]{.Repealed}

  - [(o)]{.lawlabel} means of embarkation into survival craft.

- [(1.1)]{.lawlabel} Despite subparagraph (1)(m)(i), a ship that is
  under 300 tons gross tonnage and that on March 31, 2001 was required
  by these Regulations to carry two Class II EPIRBs may continue to
  carry them instead of a SART until one of the batteries of the Class
  II EPIRBs needs to be replaced.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} A Class IX ship that is not a Safety Convention ship
  and is a tanker engaged on a near coastal voyage, Class 1, shall carry

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} where the ship is under 1,600 tons gross tonnage,
    on each side of the ship, one or more Class 1 lifeboats that are
    under davits, are 4.9 m or over in length and have an aggregate
    capacity sufficient to accommodate the complement;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} where the ship is 1,600 tons or over but under
    3,000 tons gross tonnage, the following lifeboats, under
    gravity-type davits,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} one motor lifeboat, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} on each side of the ship, one or more Class 1
      lifeboats that are 4.9 m or over in length, the aggregate capacity
      of which is sufficient to accommodate that part of the complement
      not accommodated in the motor lifeboat;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} where the ship is 3,000 tons gross tonnage or over,
    four lifeboats, under gravity-type davits,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} that are distributed equally on both sides of the
      ship, two of which are carried aft and two amidships or, where the
      ship has no amidships superstructure, are carried aft, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} one of which is a motor lifeboat or, where the
      ship is 5,000 tons gross tonnage or over, two of which are motor
      lifeboats and are carried one on each side of the ship;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} where the ship is within the length range set out
    in column I of an item of the table to this paragraph, the supply of
    equipment set out in columns II to IV of that item:

    <figure id="517912">
    <table
    class="table table-hover table-striped tablePointsize9 topdouble topbot">
    <thead>
    <tr class="topdouble">

    30.5 m</td>
    <td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom top">4</td>
    <td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom top">2</td>
    <td class="noBorderBottom top">2</td>

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} one lifejacket, fitted with a whistle and a
    personal locator light, for each member of the complement;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} one immersion suit, fitted with a whistle and a
    personal locator light, for each member of the complement;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} the following number of survival craft VHF
    radiotelephone apparatus stowed so that they are readily accessible
    for immediate use:

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} two, if the ship is 300 tons or over but under
      500 tons gross tonnage, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} three, if the ship is 500 tons gross tonnage or
      over;

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} for each life raft, a SOLAS A pack as set out in
    the LSA Code;

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2023-257, s. 451\]]{.Repealed}

  - [(j)]{.lawlabel} where the ship is 150 tons gross tonnage or over, a
    line-throwing appliance;

  - [(k)]{.lawlabel} 12 rocket parachute flares;

  - [(l)]{.lawlabel} the following SARTs:

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} if the ship is 20 m or over in length but under
      500 tons gross tonnage, one SART stowed so that it is readily
      accessible for immediate use and for placing in one of the
      survival craft that are launched first, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} if the ship is 500 tons gross tonnage or over,
      two SARTs stowed so that they are readily accessible for immediate
      use and for placing in the two survival craft that are launched
      first; and

  - [(m)]{.lawlabel} means of embarkation into survival craft.

- [(2.1)]{.lawlabel} Despite subparagraph (2)(l)(i), a ship that is
  under 300 tons gross tonnage and that on March 31, 2001 was required
  by these Regulations to carry two Class II EPIRBs may continue to
  carry them instead of a SART until one of the batteries of the Class
  II EPIRBs needs to be replaced.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} Notwithstanding paragraphs (1)(c) and (2)(c), where
  it is not practicable to carry four lifeboats aft, a Class IX ship may
  carry two lifeboats, one on each side of the ship, if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} each lifeboat

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} has the capacity sufficient to accommodate the
      complement and is not over

      - [(A)]{.lawlabel} 8.5 m in length, where the ship is engaged on
        an unlimited voyage, or

      - [(B)]{.lawlabel} 7.9 m in length, where the ship is engaged on a
        near coastal voyage, Class 1, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} is stowed

      - [(A)]{.lawlabel} as near as is safe and practicable to the
        waterline of the ship in its lightest seagoing condition, and

      - [(B)]{.lawlabel} as far forward as is practicable and, in any
        case, at least 1.5 times its own length forward of the ship's
        propeller; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} life rafts having the capacity sufficient to
    accommodate 50 per cent of the complement are carried.

#### [Signs]{.HTitleText3} {#h-517945 .Subheading}

**[[22.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-22.2 .sectionLabel}** A Class IX ship shall
be provided with signs that indicate

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the location of

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} survival craft and their launching devices, and

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} embarkation stations; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} directions to the embarkation stations.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-218, s. 20
:::

### [Class X Ships]{.HTitleText2}[(Ships that are over 15 tons gross tonnage, are not Safety Convention ships and are not certified to carry passengers, or are certified to carry 12 or fewer passengers, on near coastal voyages, Class 1, limited to 120 nautical miles from shore, near coastal voyages, Class 2, sheltered waters voyages, inland voyages, inland voyages limited to 25 nautical miles from shore, or near coastal voyages, Class 2, limited to five nautical miles from shore)]{.HTitleText2} {#h-517953 .Subheading}

::: HistoricalNote
\[

- SOR/2023-257, s. 452

\]
:::

#### [Ships Other Than Tankers]{.HTitleText3} {#h-517954 .Subheading}

::: HistoricalNote
\[

- SOR/96-218, s. 21

\]
:::

**[[23]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-23 .sectionLabel}** Subject to section 27.1,
a Class X ship that is engaged on a near coastal voyage, Class 1,
limited to 120 nautical miles from shore, other than a tanker, shall
carry

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} if the ship is 30.5 m or more in length,

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} on each side thereof, one or more Class 1 lifeboats
    each of not less than 3.54 m^3^ capacity, each under davits, and of
    sufficient aggregate capacity to accommodate the complement,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} on each side thereof, one or more Class 1
    lifeboats each of not less than 3.54 m^3^ capacity, each under
    davits, and one or more life rafts that together with the lifeboats
    on either side have sufficient capacity to accommodate the
    complement, the aggregate capacity of the life rafts and lifeboats
    being sufficient to accommodate double the complement,

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} on one side thereof, one or more Class 1
    lifeboats each of not less than 3.54 m^3^ capacity, each under
    davits, and one or more life rafts that together with the lifeboats
    have sufficient capacity to accommodate double the complement, the
    life rafts alone being capable of accommodating the complement, or

  - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} in addition to two or more equal-sized life rafts,
    a Class 2 lifeboat or approved boat that has a capacity of not less
    than 3.54 m^3^ and a means of launching, the aggregate capacity of
    those survival craft being sufficient to accommodate 200 per cent of
    the complement;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} if such ship is over 18.3 m but under 30.5 m in
  length, either

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} on each side one or more Class 1 lifeboats, each of
    at least 2.12 m^3^ capacity, each under davits and of sufficient
    aggregate capacity to accommodate the complement,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} on each side one or more Class 1 lifeboats, each
    of at least 2.12 m^3^ capacity, each under davits, and one or more
    life rafts that together with the lifeboats on either side have
    sufficient capacity to accommodate the complement, the aggregate
    capacity of the life rafts and lifeboats being sufficient to
    accommodate 1.5 times the complement,

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} one Class 1 lifeboat of not less than 2.12 m^3^
    capacity, capable of being launched on either side of the ship and
    of sufficient capacity to accommodate the complement, or

  - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} a Class 2 lifeboat or approved boat that has a
    means of launching and the capacity to accommodate no fewer than
    four persons, in addition to two or more equal-sized life rafts, the
    aggregate capacity of those survival craft being sufficient to
    accommodate 150 per cent of the complement;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/96-218, s. 22\]]{.Repealed}

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} if such ship is 18.3 m in length or under,

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} one Class 1 lifeboat of at least 1.61 m^3^
    capacity, capable of being launched on either side of the ship, and
    of sufficient capacity to accommodate the complement,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} a Class 2 lifeboat or suitable boat that has a
    means of launching and the capacity to accommodate no fewer than
    four persons, and one or more life rafts, the aggregate capacity of
    those survival craft being sufficient to accommodate 150 per cent of
    the complement and that of the life rafts alone being sufficient to
    accommodate the complement, or

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a ship other than a tug, two or
    more equal size life rafts of aggregate capacity sufficient to
    accommodate double the complement;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} if such ship is a tug, in addition to the other
  requirements of this section, one or more life rafts with sufficient
  aggregate capacity to accommodate the complement but any life rafts
  provided to meet the requirements of paragraph (a), (b) or (d) may be
  counted toward the requirement of this paragraph; and

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} a Class 1 motor lifeboat of at least 3.54 m^3^
  capacity, under davits that may count towards the lifeboat
  requirements elsewhere in this section, but this paragraph does not
  apply to

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a ship of less than 3,000 tons, gross tonnage, or

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} a ship that does not go beyond the Gulf of St.
    Lawrence.

- [(g) to (n)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/96-218, s. 22\]]{.Repealed}

<!-- -->

- [(2) to (4)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/96-218, s. 22\]]{.Repealed}

**[[24]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-24 .sectionLabel}** Subject to section 27.1,
a Class X ship that is engaged on a near coastal voyage, Class 2, other
than a tanker, shall carry

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} if such ship is 30.5 m in length or over,

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} on each side thereof, one or more Class 1
    lifeboats, each of at least 2.12 m^3^ capacity, each under davits,
    and of sufficient aggregate capacity to accommodate the complement,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} on each side thereof, one or more Class 1
    lifeboats, each of at least 2.12 m^3^ capacity, each under davits,
    and one or more life rafts that together with the lifeboats on
    either side have sufficient capacity to accommodate the complement,
    the aggregate capacity of the life rafts and lifeboats being
    sufficient to accommodate 1.5 times the complement, or

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} in addition to one or more life rafts, a Class 2
    lifeboat or approved boat that has a capacity of not less than 2.12
    m^3^ and a means of launching, the aggregate capacity of those
    survival craft being sufficient to accommodate 150 per cent of the
    complement and that of the life rafts alone being sufficient to
    accommodate the complement,

  but where a non-passenger ship carries more than one lifeboat, one
  lifeboat may be a Class 2 lifeboat;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} if such ship is over 18.3 m but under 30.5 m in
  length,

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} on each side one or more Class 1 lifeboats, each of
    at least 2.12 m^3^ capacity, each under davits and of sufficient
    aggregate capacity to accommodate the complement,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} on each side one or more Class 1 lifeboats, each
    of at least 2.12 m^3^ capacity, each under davits, and one or more
    life rafts that together with the lifeboats on either side have
    sufficient capacity to accommodate the complement, the aggregate
    capacity of the life rafts and lifeboats being sufficient to
    accommodate 1.5 times the complement,

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} one Class 1 lifeboat of at least 2.12 m^3^
    capacity, capable of being launched on either side of the ship and
    of sufficient capacity to accommodate the complement, or

  - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} in addition to one or more life rafts, a Class 2
    lifeboat or approved boat that has the capacity to accommodate no
    fewer than four persons, the aggregate capacity of those survival
    craft being sufficient to accommodate 150 per cent of the complement
    and that of the life rafts alone being sufficient to accommodate the
    complement,

  but where a non-passenger ship carries more than one lifeboat, one
  lifeboat may be Class 2;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} if such ship is 18.3 m in length or under,

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} one Class 1 lifeboat of at least 1.42 m^3^
    capacity, capable of being launched on either side of the ship and
    of sufficient capacity to accommodate the complement,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} a suitable boat and one or more life rafts, the
    aggregate capacity of those survival craft being sufficient to
    accommodate the complement, or

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a ship other than a tug, two or
    more equal size life rafts of aggregate capacity sufficient to
    accommodate double the complement; and

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} if such ship is a tug, in addition to the other
  requirements of this section, one or more life rafts with sufficient
  aggregate capacity to accommodate the complement, but any life rafts
  provided to meet the requirements of paragraph (a), (b) or (c) may be
  counted toward the requirement of this paragraph.

- [(e) to (k)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/96-218, s. 24\]]{.Repealed}

<!-- -->

- [(2) and (3)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/96-218, s. 24\]]{.Repealed}

**[[25]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-25 .sectionLabel}** Subject to section 27.1,
a Class X ship that is engaged on an inland voyage, other than a tanker,
shall carry

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} if the ship is 30.5 m in length or over, either

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} on each side thereof, one or more Class 1
    lifeboats, each of at least 3.54 m^3^ capacity, each under davits
    and of sufficient aggregate capacity to accommodate the complement,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} on each side thereof, one or more Class 1
    lifeboats, each of at least 3.54 m^3^ capacity, each under davits,
    and one or more life rafts that together with the lifeboats on
    either side have sufficient capacity to accommodate the complement,
    the aggregate capacity of the life rafts and lifeboats being
    sufficient to accommodate double the complement, or

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} in addition to two or more life rafts, a Class 2
    lifeboat or approved boat that has a capacity of not less than 3.54
    m^3^ and a means of launching, the aggregate capacity of those
    survival craft being sufficient to accommodate 200 per cent of the
    complement and that of the life rafts alone being sufficient to
    accommodate the complement,

  but where a non-passenger ship carries more than one lifeboat, one
  lifeboat may be a Class 2 lifeboat;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} where the distance from any accommodation to the
  nearest equipment required under paragraph (a) exceeds 91.4 m, one or
  more life rafts readily available to and sufficient to accommodate all
  persons housed in such accommodation, but this equipment need not be
  additional to life rafts already carried in accordance with paragraph
  (a);

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} if such ship is over 18.3 m but under 30.5 m in
  length,

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} on each side thereof, one or more Class 1
    lifeboats, each of at least 2.12 m^3^ capacity, each under davits,
    and of sufficient aggregate capacity to accommodate the complement,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} on each side thereof, one or more Class 1
    lifeboats, each of at least 2.12 m^3^ capacity, each under davits,
    and one or more life rafts that together with the lifeboats on
    either side have sufficient capacity to accommodate the complement,
    the aggregate capacity of the life rafts and lifeboats being
    sufficient to accommodate 1.5 times the complement,

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} one Class 1 lifeboat of not less than 2.12 m^3^
    capacity, capable of being launched on either side of the ship and
    of sufficient capacity to accommodate the complement, or

  - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} in addition to one or more life rafts, a Class 2
    lifeboat or approved boat that has a means of launching and the
    capacity to accommodate no fewer than four persons, the aggregate
    capacity of those survival craft being sufficient to accommodate 150
    per cent of the complement and that of the life rafts alone being
    sufficient to accommodate the complement,

  but where a non-passenger ship carries more than one lifeboat, one
  lifeboat may be Class 2;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} if such ship is 18.3 m in length or under,

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} one Class 1 lifeboat of at least 1.42 m^3^
    capacity, capable of being launched on either side of the ship and
    of sufficient capacity to accommodate the complement,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} a Class 2 lifeboat or suitable boat that has a
    capacity of not less than 1.42 m^3^ and a means of launching and one
    or more life rafts, the aggregate capacity of those survival craft
    being sufficient to accommodate the complement, or

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a ship other than a tug, two or
    more equal size life rafts of aggregate capacity sufficient to
    accommodate double the complement; and

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} if such ship is a tug, in addition to the other
  requirements of this section, one or more life rafts with sufficient
  aggregate capacity to accommodate the complement, but any life rafts
  provided to meet the requirements of paragraph (a), (c) or (d), may be
  counted toward the requirement of this paragraph.

- [(f) to (l)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/96-218, s. 26\]]{.Repealed}

**[[26]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-26 .sectionLabel}** Subject to section 27.1,
a Class X ship that is engaged on an inland voyage, limited to 25
nautical miles from shore, or a near coastal voyage, Class 2, limited to
five nautical miles from shore, other than a tanker, shall carry

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} if such ship is 30.5 m in length or over,

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} on each side thereof, one or more Class 1
    lifeboats, each of at least 2.12 m^3^ capacity, each under davits
    and of sufficient aggregate capacity to accommodate the complement,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} on each side thereof, one or more Class 1
    lifeboats, each of at least 2.12 m^3^ capacity, each under davits,
    and one or more life rafts that together with the lifeboats on
    either side have sufficient capacity to accommodate the complement,
    the aggregate capacity of the life rafts and lifeboats being
    sufficient to accommodate 1.5 times the complement, or

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} in addition to one or more life rafts, a Class 2
    lifeboat or approved boat that has a capacity of not less than 2.12
    m^3^ and a means of launching, the aggregate capacity of those
    survival craft being sufficient to accommodate 150 per cent of the
    complement and that of the life rafts alone being sufficient to
    accommodate the complement,

  but where a non-passenger ship carries more than one lifeboat, one
  lifeboat may be Class 2;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} where the distance from any accommodation to the
  nearest equipment required under paragraph (a) exceeds 91.4 m, life
  rafts readily available to and sufficient to accommodate all persons
  housed in such accommodation, but this equipment need not be
  additional to life rafts already carried in accordance with paragraph
  (a);

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} if such ship is over 18.3 m but under 30.5 m in
  length,

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} on each side thereof, one or more Class 1
    lifeboats, each of at least 2.12 m^3^ capacity, each under davits
    and of sufficient aggregate capacity to accommodate the complement,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} on each side thereof, one or more Class 1
    lifeboats, each of at least 2.12 m^3^ capacity, each under davits,
    and one or more life rafts that together with the lifeboats on
    either side have sufficient capacity to accommodate the complement,

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} one Class 1 lifeboat of at least 2.12 m^3^
    capacity, capable of being launched on either side of the ship and
    of sufficient capacity to accommodate the complement, or

  - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} in addition to one or more life rafts, a Class 2
    lifeboat or approved boat that has a means of launching and the
    capacity to accommodate no fewer than four persons, the aggregate
    capacity of those survival craft being sufficient to accommodate the
    complement,

  but where a non-passenger ship carries more than one lifeboat, one
  lifeboat may be Class 2;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} if such ship is 18.3 m in length or under, either

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} one Class 1 lifeboat of at least 1.42 m^3^
    capacity, capable of being launched on either side of the ship and
    of sufficient capacity to accommodate the complement,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} a suitable boat and one or more life rafts, the
    aggregate capacity of those survival craft being sufficient to
    accommodate the complement, or

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a ship other than a tug, two or
    more equal size life rafts of aggregate capacity sufficient to
    accommodate double the complement; and

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} if such ship is a tug, in addition to the other
  requirements of this section, one or more life rafts with sufficient
  aggregate capacity to accommodate the complement, but any life rafts
  provided to meet the requirements of paragraph (a), (c) or (d) may be
  counted toward the requirement of this paragraph.

- [(f) to (k)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/96-218, s. 28\]]{.Repealed}

**[[27]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-27 .sectionLabel}** Subject to section 27.1,
a Class X ship that is engaged on a sheltered waters voyage, other than
a tanker, shall carry

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} if such ship is 18.3 m in length or over,

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} one or more Class 2 lifeboats, each of at least
    1.42 m^3^ capacity, each with means of launching and of sufficient
    aggregate capacity to accommodate the complement,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} a Class 2 lifeboat that has a capacity of not less
    than 1.42 m^3^ and a means of launching, and one or more life rafts
    or inflatable rescue platforms, the aggregate capacity of those
    survival craft being sufficient to accommodate the complement, or

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} one or more life rafts sufficient for the
    complement;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} if such ship is under 18.3 m in length,

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} one or more Class 2 lifeboats, each of at least
    1.42 m^3^ capacity, each with means of launching and of sufficient
    aggregate capacity to accommodate the complement,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} a suitable boat and one or more life rafts or
    inflatable rescue platforms, the aggregate capacity of those
    survival craft being sufficient to accommodate the complement or,
    where the carriage of a suitable boat is impracticable, sufficient
    life rafts or inflatable rescue platforms to accommodate the
    complement, or

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} one or more life rafts sufficient for the
    complement; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} if such ship is a tug with a complement of two or
  more, in addition to the other requirements of this section, one or
  more life rafts with sufficient aggregate capacity to accommodate the
  complement, but any life rafts provided to meet the requirements of
  paragraph (a) or (b) may be counted toward the requirement of this
  paragraph.

- [(d) to (g)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/96-218, s. 30\]]{.Repealed}

<!-- -->

- [(2) and (3)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/96-218, s. 30\]]{.Repealed}

- **[[27.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-27.1 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  Class X ship, other than a tanker, that is a bulk carrier, is over
  91.4 m in length and is engaged on a voyage on the St. Lawrence River
  east of the Montreal entrance to the St. Lawrence Seaway shall include
  in its life saving equipment life rafts of sufficient capacity to
  accommodate 50 per cent of the complement.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where some of the persons carried on a ship referred
  to in subsection (1) are berthed in the forward part of the ship, life
  rafts of sufficient capacity to accommodate all of those persons shall
  be stowed forward and the remaining life rafts shall be stowed aft.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-218, s. 31
:::

- **[[27.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-27.2 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  Class X ship, other than a tanker or a tug, that is engaged on a
  voyage set out in column I of an item of the table to this subsection
  and is within the length range set out in column II of that item shall
  carry the supply of equipment set out in columns III to V of that
  item.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} A Class X ship that is a tug shall carry

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} if the tug is 25 m in length or over,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} two lifebuoys fitted with self-igniting lights,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} two lifebuoys fitted with buoyant lifelines, and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} two additional lifebuoys; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if the tug is less than 25 m in length,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} two lifebuoys that are arranged so as to float
      free if the tug suddenly capsizes,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} one lifebuoy fitted with a self-igniting light,
      and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} one lifebuoy fitted with a buoyant lifeline.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} A Class X ship, other than a tanker, shall carry

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the following supply of lifejackets, each fitted
    with a whistle and a personal locator light:

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} one for each member of the complement, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} where the ship is a tug, at least two stowed in
      the wheelhouse and two stowed in the engine room;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} where the ship is engaged on a voyage other than a
    sheltered waters voyage, one immersion suit, fitted with a whistle
    and a personal locator light, for each member of the complement;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the following number of survival craft VHF
    radiotelephone apparatus stowed so that they are readily accessible
    for immediate use:

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} two, in the case of a ship that is 300 tons or
      over but under 500 tons gross tonnage and is engaged on a near
      coastal voyage, Class 1, limited to 120 nautical miles from shore,
      or a near coastal voyage, Class 2, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} three, in the case of a ship that is 500 tons
      gross tonnage or over and is engaged on a near coastal voyage,
      Class 1, limited to 120 nautical miles from shore, or a near
      coastal voyage, Class 2; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} for each life raft,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} where the ship is engaged on a near coastal
      voyage, Class 1, limited to 120 nautical miles from shore, a SOLAS
      A pack as set out in the LSA Code, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} in any other case, a SOLAS B pack as set out in
      the LSA Code;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} for each approved boat, the equipment set out in
    section 2 of Schedule II;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} for each suitable boat, the equipment set out in
    section 5 of Schedule II;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} if the ship is 500 tons gross tonnage or over and
    is engaged on a voyage other than a sheltered waters voyage, a
    line-throwing appliance;

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} the following distress signals:

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} if the ship is under 85 m in length, 12
      pyrotechnic distress signals of which six are rocket parachute
      flares, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} if the ship is 85 m or over in length, 12 rocket
      parachute flares;

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} means of embarkation into survival craft, except
    where the ship is engaged on a sheltered waters voyage; and

  - [(j)]{.lawlabel} the following SARTs:

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a ship that is 300 tons or over
      but under 500 tons gross tonnage and is engaged on a voyage beyond
      the VHF coverage area or sea area A1, one SART stowed so that it
      is readily accessible for immediate use and for placing in one of
      the survival craft that are launched first, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a ship that is 500 tons gross
      tonnage or over and is engaged on a voyage beyond the VHF coverage
      area or sea area A1, two SARTs stowed so that they are readily
      accessible for immediate use and for placing in the two survival
      craft that are launched first.

#### [Tankers]{.HTitleText3} {#h-518111 .Subheading}

**[[27.3]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-27.3 .sectionLabel}** A Class X ship that
is a tanker shall carry

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} where the ship is under 1,600 tons gross tonnage, on
  each side of the ship, one or more Class 1 lifeboats under davits,
  each of which has a capacity of not less than 3.54 m^3^ and the
  aggregate capacity of which is sufficient to accommodate the
  complement;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} where the ship is 1,600 tons or over but under 3,000
  tons gross tonnage, the following lifeboats, under gravity-type
  davits,

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} one motor lifeboat, and

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} on each side of the ship, one or more Class 1
    lifeboats that are 4.9 m in length or over, the aggregate capacity
    of which is sufficient to accommodate that part of the complement
    not accommodated in the motor lifeboat;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} where the ship is 3,000 tons gross tonnage or over,
  four lifeboats, under gravity-type davits,

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} that are distributed equally on both sides of the
    ship, two of which are carried aft and two amidships or, where the
    ship has no amidships superstructure, are carried aft, and

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} one of which is a motor lifeboat or, where the
    ship is 5,000 tons gross tonnage or over, two of which are motor
    lifeboats and are carried one on each side of the ship;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} where the ship is within the length range set out in
  column I of an item of the table to this paragraph, the supply of
  equipment set out in columns II to IV of that item:

  <figure id="518121">
  <table
  class="table table-hover table-striped tablePointsize9 topdouble topbot">
  <thead>
  <tr class="topdouble">
  <th class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom top"></th>
  <th class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom top" scope="col">Column I</th>
  <th class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom top" scope="col">Column II</th>
  <th class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom top" scope="col">Column III</th>
  <th class="noBorderBottom top" scope="col">Column IV</th>
  </tr>
  <tr>
  <th class="noBorderRight borderBottom bottom" scope="col">Item</th>
  <th class="noBorderRight borderBottom bottom" scope="col">Length of
  Ship</th>
  <th class="noBorderRight borderBottom bottom" scope="col">Lifebuoys</th>
  <th class="noBorderRight borderBottom bottom" scope="col">Self-igniting
  Lights</th>
  <th class="borderBottom bottom" scope="col">Buoyant Lifelines</th>
  </tr>
  </thead>
  <tbody>
  <tr>
  <td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom top">1</td>
  <td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom top" scope="row">Under
  30.5 m</td>
  <td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom top">2</td>
  <td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom top">1</td>
  <td class="noBorderBottom top">1</td>
  </tr>
  <tr>
  <td class="noBorderRight top">2</td>
  <td class="noBorderRight top" scope="row">30.5 m or over</td>

  <td class="top">2</td>
  </tr>

  <figcaption><p>TABLE</p></figcaption>
  </figure>

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} one lifejacket, fitted with a whistle and a personal
  locator light, for each member of the complement;

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} one immersion suit, fitted with a whistle and a
  personal locator light, for each member of the complement;

- [(g)]{.lawlabel} the following number of survival craft VHF
  radiotelephone apparatus stowed so that they are readily accessible
  for immediate use:

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} two, in the case of a ship that is 300 tons or over
    but under 500 tons gross tonnage and is engaged on a near coastal
    voyage, Class 1, limited to 120 nautical miles from shore, or a near
    coastal voyage, Class 2, and

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} three, in the case of a ship that is 500 tons
    gross tonnage or over and is engaged on a near coastal voyage, Class
    1, limited to 120 nautical miles from shore, or a near coastal
    voyage, Class 2;

- [(h)]{.lawlabel} for each life raft,

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} if the ship is engaged on a sheltered waters voyage
    or a voyage that does not go beyond the Gulf of St. Lawrence, a
    SOLAS B pack as set out in the LSA Code, and

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} in any other case, a SOLAS A pack as set out in
    the LSA Code;

- [(i)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2023-257, s. 454\]]{.Repealed}

- [(j)]{.lawlabel} if the ship is 500 tons gross tonnage or over and is
  engaged on a voyage other than a sheltered waters voyage, a
  line-throwing appliance;

- [(k)]{.lawlabel} 12 rocket parachute flares;

- [(l)]{.lawlabel} means of embarkation into survival craft, except
  where the ship is engaged on a sheltered waters voyage; and

- [(m)]{.lawlabel} the following SARTs:

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a ship that is 300 tons or over but
    under 500 tons gross tonnage and is engaged on a voyage beyond the
    VHF coverage area or sea area A1, one SART stowed so that it is
    readily accessible for immediate use and for placing in one of the
    survival craft that are launched first, and

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a ship that is 500 tons gross
    tonnage or over and is engaged on a voyage beyond the VHF coverage
    area or sea area A1, two SARTs stowed so that they are readily
    accessible for immediate use and for placing in the two survival
    craft that are launched first.

#### [Signs]{.HTitleText3} {#h-518147 .Subheading}

**[[27.4]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-27.4 .sectionLabel}** A Class X ship shall
be provided with signs that indicate

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the location of

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} survival craft and their launching devices, and

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} embarkation stations; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} directions to the embarkation stations.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-218, s. 31
:::

### [Class XI Ships]{.HTitleText2}[(Ships that are over 15 tons gross tonnage, are not certified to carry passengers but carry a crew, are not self-propelled and are towed or pushed by a ship or operated by a cable)]{.HTitleText2} {#h-518155 .Subheading}

::: HistoricalNote
\[

- SOR/96-218, s. 32

\]
:::

- **[[28]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-28 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  Class XI ship that is engaged on a voyage more than 20 nautical miles
  from shore shall carry

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} if the ship is 30.5 m in length or over,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} one or more Class 1 lifeboats with means of
      launching, each of not less than 3.54 m^3^ capacity, and of
      sufficient aggregate capacity to accommodate the complement,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} one or more Class 1 lifeboats with means of
      launching, each of not less than 3.54 m^3^ capacity, and one or
      more life rafts that together with the lifeboats have sufficient
      capacity to accommodate the complement, or

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} in addition to one or more life rafts, a Class
      2 lifeboat or approved boat that has a capacity of not less than
      3.54 m^3^ and a means of launching, the aggregate capacity of
      those survival craft being sufficient to accommodate the
      complement;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if the ship is over 18.3 m but under 30.5 m in
    length,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} one or more Class 1 lifeboats with means of
      launching, each of not less than 2.12 m^3^ capacity, and of
      sufficient aggregate capacity to accommodate the complement,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} one or more Class 1 lifeboats with means of
      launching, each of not less than 2.12 m^3^ capacity, and one or
      more life rafts that together with the lifeboats have sufficient
      capacity to accommodate the complement, or

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} in addition to one or more life rafts, a Class
      2 lifeboat or approved boat that has a means of launching and the
      capacity to accommodate no fewer than four persons, the aggregate
      capacity of those survival craft being sufficient to accommodate
      the complement; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} if the ship is 18.3 m in length or under,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a Class 1 lifeboat with means of launching, of
      not less than 1.61 m^3^ capacity, and of sufficient capacity to
      accommodate the complement,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} a Class 1 lifeboat with means of launching, of
      not less than 1.61 m^3^ capacity, and one or more life rafts that
      together with the lifeboat have sufficient capacity to accommodate
      the complement, or

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} a Class 2 lifeboat or suitable boat that has a
      means of launching, and one or more life rafts, the aggregate
      capacity of those survival craft being sufficient to accommodate
      the complement.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (3), a Class XI ship that is
  engaged on a voyage not more than 20 nautical miles from shore shall
  carry

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} if the ship is 30.5 m in length or over,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} one or more Class 1 lifeboats with means of
      launching, each of not less than 2.12 m^3^ capacity, and of
      sufficient aggregate capacity to accommodate the complement,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} one or more Class 1 lifeboats with means of
      launching, each of not less than 2.12 m^3^ capacity, and one or
      more life rafts that together with the lifeboats have sufficient
      capacity to accommodate the complement, or

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} in addition to one or more life rafts, a Class
      2 lifeboat or approved boat that has a capacity of not less than
      2.12 m^3^ and a means of launching, the aggregate capacity of
      those survival craft being sufficient to accommodate the
      complement;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if the ship is over 18.3 m but under 30.5 m in
    length,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} one or more Class 1 lifeboats with means of
      launching, each of not less than 2.12 m^3^ capacity, and of
      sufficient aggregate capacity to accommodate the complement,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} one or more Class 1 lifeboats with means of
      launching, each of not less than 2.12 m^3^ capacity, and one or
      more life rafts that together with the lifeboats have sufficient
      capacity to accommodate the complement, or

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} in addition to one or more life rafts, a Class
      2 lifeboat or approved boat that has a means of launching and the
      capacity to accommodate no fewer than four persons, the aggregate
      capacity of those survival craft being sufficient to accommodate
      the complement; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} if the ship is 18.3 m in length or under,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a Class 1 lifeboat with means of launching, of
      not less than 1.42 m^3^ capacity, and of sufficient capacity to
      accommodate the complement,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} a Class 1 lifeboat with means of launching, of
      not less than 1.42 m^3^ capacity, and one or more life rafts that
      together with the lifeboat have sufficient capacity to accommodate
      the complement, or

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} a Class 2 lifeboat or suitable boat that has a
      means of launching, and one or more life rafts, the aggregate
      capacity of those survival craft being sufficient to accommodate
      the complement.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} A Class XI ship that engages solely on sheltered
  waters voyages, shall carry

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} one or more Class 2 lifeboats with means of
    launching, each of not less than 1.42 m^3^ capacity and of
    sufficient aggregate capacity to accommodate the complement; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a Class 2 lifeboat or suitable boat with a means of
    launching, and one or more life rafts of sufficient capacity to
    accommodate the complement.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} In addition to meeting the requirements of
  subsections (1) to (3), a Class XI ship that carries its survival
  craft more than 100 m from an area where persons are berthed shall
  carry, readily available to the persons berthed there, enough life
  rafts to accommodate them.

  an item of the table to this subsection shall carry the supply of
  equipment set out in columns II to IV of that item.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Notwithstanding the requirements of subsection (1), a
  Class XI ship need not carry more lifebuoys than there are members of
  the complement.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-218, s. 34
:::

**[[30]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-30 .sectionLabel}** A Class XI ship shall
carry

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} one lifejacket, fitted with a whistle and a personal
  locator light, for each member of the complement;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} where the ship is engaged on a voyage other than a
  sheltered waters voyage, one immersion suit, fitted with a whistle and
  a personal locator light, for each member of the complement;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} where the ship is engaged on a voyage more than 20
  nautical miles from shore, a loud hailer or an equally efficient means
  of communication with the complement;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} for each life raft,

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} if the ship is engaged on a near coastal voyage,
    Class 1, or a near coastal voyage, Class 1, limited to 120 nautical
    miles from shore, beyond the Gulf of St. Lawrence, a SOLAS A pack as
    set out in the LSA Code, and

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} in any other case, a SOLAS B pack as set out in
    the LSA Code;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} for each approved boat, the equipment set out in
  section 2 of Schedule II;

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} for each suitable boat, the equipment set out in
  section 5 of Schedule II;

- [(g)]{.lawlabel} one line-throwing appliance, unless the ship

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} is engaged on a sheltered waters voyage, or

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} is making a voyage solely under tow and the tow
    boat is equipped with a line-throwing appliance; and

- [(h)]{.lawlabel} six rocket parachute flares.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-218, s. 34
- SOR/2001-179, s. 25
- [SOR/2023-257, s. 455](#1431320-1435766){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1431320-1435766.html"
  role="button"}
- [SOR/2023-257, s. 505](#1431444-1436373){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1431444-1436373.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-30-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 30"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Signs]{.HTitleText3} {#h-518222 .Subheading}

**[[31]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-31 .sectionLabel}** A Class XI ship shall be
provided with signs that indicate

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the location of

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} survival craft and their launching devices, and

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} embarkation stations; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} directions to the embarkation stations.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-218, s. 34
:::

## [PART II]{.HLabel1}[Equipment to Be Carried by New Ships]{.HTitleText1} {#h-518230 .Part}

### [Class I Ships]{.HTitleText2}[(Ships that are over five tons gross tonnage and are (i) Safety Convention ships that are certified to carry more than 12 passengers on unlimited voyages, or (ii) ships that are not Safety Convention ships and that are certified to carry more than 12 passengers on unlimited voyages or near coastal voyages, Class 1)]{.HTitleText2} {#h-518231 .Subheading}

::: HistoricalNote
\[

- SOR/2023-357, s. 456

\]
:::

- **[[32]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-32
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to section 33, a Class I
  ship shall carry, on each side of the ship,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} enough lifeboats under gravity-type davits to
    accommodate at least 50 per cent of the complement; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} enough lifeboats under gravity-type davits to
    accommodate at least 37.5 per cent of the complement and enough life
    rafts under launching devices to accommodate at least 12.5 per cent
    of the complement.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} In addition to meeting the requirements of subsection
  (1), a Class I ship shall carry enough life rafts under launching
  devices to accommodate at least 25 per cent of the complement.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} Lifeboats shall be partially enclosed or totally
  enclosed.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-218, s. 34
:::

- **[[33]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-33
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (3), a Class I
  ship that is under 500 tons gross tonnage with a complement of fewer
  than 200 persons may carry, on each side of the ship, instead of the
  survival craft referred to in section 32, enough life rafts to
  accommodate

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the complement, if the life rafts are stowed in a
    location providing for easy side-to-side transfer at a single
    open-deck level; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} 150 per cent of the complement, if the life rafts
    are not stowed in accordance with paragraph (a).

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where a rescue boat required under subsection 34(2)
  is certified as a lifeboat, its accommodation capacity may be included
  in the calculation under subsection (1) to reduce the number of life
  rafts required.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} The number of life rafts necessary to fulfil the
  requirements of subsection (1) shall be calculated in such a way that,
  taking into account the percentage of the complement that could be
  accommodated in each, should any one life raft be lost or rendered
  unserviceable, enough life rafts remain on each side of the ship to
  accommodate the complement.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-218, s. 34
:::

- **[[34]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-34 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  Class I ship of 500 tons gross tonnage or over shall carry, on each
  side of the ship, at least one rescue boat under launching devices.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} A Class I ship of under 500 tons gross tonnage shall
  carry at least one rescue boat under launching devices.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-218, s. 34
:::

**[[35]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-35 .sectionLabel}** Every life raft on a
Class I ship shall be stowed

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} with its painter permanently attached to the ship;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} fitted with a float-free device;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} so that it can be launched by one or two persons; and

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} if it is inflatable, so that it inflates
  automatically if the ship sinks.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-218, s. 34
:::

**[[36]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-36 .sectionLabel}** A Class I ship shall
carry enough lifeboats and rescue boats to ensure that, in providing for
abandonment by the complement, each lifeboat or rescue boat need not
marshal more than six life rafts.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-218, s. 34
:::

**[[37]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-37 .sectionLabel}** A Class I ship within the
length range set out in column I of an item of the table to this section
shall carry the supply of equipment set out in columns II to V of that
item.

- SOR/96-218, s. 34
:::

**[[38]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-38 .sectionLabel}** A Class I ship shall
carry

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} one lifejacket, fitted with a whistle and a personal
  locator light, for each member of the complement; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the following supply of lifejackets, each fitted with
  a whistle and a personal locator light:

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} enough conspicuously stowed on deck for 5 per cent
    of the complement,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} enough that are suitable for children for at least
    10 per cent of the complement or one for each child on board,
    whichever is greater,

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} enough for persons on watch, and

  - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} enough at locations that are remote from muster
    and embarkation stations for all of the persons likely to be there.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-218, s. 34
- SOR/2004-26, s. 13
:::

**[[39]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-39 .sectionLabel}** A Class I ship shall
carry

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} three survival craft VHF radiotelephone apparatus
  stowed so that they are readily accessible for immediate use;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} a marine anti-exposure work suit for each member of
  the rescue boat crew;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} for each life raft, a SOLAS A pack as set out in the
  LSA Code;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2023-257, s. 457\]]{.Repealed}

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2023-257, s. 457\]]{.Repealed}

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} a line-throwing appliance;

- [(g)]{.lawlabel} 12 rocket parachute flares;

- [(h)]{.lawlabel} one portable battery-powered loud hailer located at
  each muster station where

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} more than 100 persons are to be mustered, or

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} a marine evacuation system is used; and

- [(i)]{.lawlabel} means of embarkation into survival craft.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-218, s. 34
- SOR/2000-261, s. 13
- SOR/2001-179, ss. 26, 76(F)
- [SOR/2023-257, s. 457](#1431327-1435775){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1431327-1435775.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-39-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 39"){.PITLink}
:::

**[[40]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-40 .sectionLabel}** A Class I ship that is a
Safety Convention ship or is 20 m or over in length shall carry two
SARTs stowed so that they are readily accessible for immediate use and
for placing in the two survival craft that are launched first.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-218, s. 34
- SOR/2000-261, s. 14
:::

**[[41]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-41 .sectionLabel}** A Class I ship shall be
provided with signs that indicate

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the location of

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} survival craft and their launching devices,

  - [(i.1)]{.lawlabel} lifejackets and lifejackets suitable for
    children,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} muster stations, and

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} embarkation stations; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} directions to the muster and embarkation stations.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-218, s. 34
- SOR/2004-26, s. 14
:::

### [Class II Ships]{.HTitleText2}[(Ships that are over five tons gross tonnage and are (i) Safety Convention ships that are certified to carry more than 12 passengers on short international voyages, or (ii) ships that are not Safety Convention ships and that are certified to carry more than 12 passengers on near coastal voyages, Class 1, limited to 120 nautical miles from shore)]{.HTitleText2} {#h-518307 .Subheading}

::: HistoricalNote
\[

- SOR/2023-257, s. 499

\]
:::

- **[[42]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-42
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to section 43, a Class II
  ship that is a Safety Convention ship shall carry

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} on each side of the ship, enough lifeboats under
    gravity-type davits to accommodate at least 15 per cent of the
    complement; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} equally distributed on each side of the ship,
    insofar as it is practicable, enough life rafts under launching
    devices to accommodate that part of the complement not accommodated
    in the lifeboats.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subject to section 43, every Class II ship that is
  not a Safety Convention ship shall carry, equally distributed on each
  side of the ship, insofar as it is practicable, enough of the
  following to accommodate the complement:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} lifeboats under gravity-type davits; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a combination of life rafts and lifeboats all of
    which are under launching devices.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} In addition to meeting the requirements of
  subsection (1) or (2), a Class II ship shall carry enough life rafts
  under launching devices to accommodate at least 25 per cent of the
  complement.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} Lifeboats shall be partially enclosed or totally
  enclosed.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-218, s. 34
:::

- **[[43]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-43
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (3), a Class
  II ship that is under 500 tons gross tonnage with a complement of
  fewer than 200 persons may carry on each side of the ship, instead of
  the survival craft referred to in section 42, enough life rafts to
  accommodate

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the complement, if the life rafts are stowed in a
    location providing for easy side-to-side transfer at a single
    open-deck level; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} 150 per cent of the complement, if the life rafts
    are not stowed in accordance with paragraph (a).

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where a rescue boat required under subsection 44(2)
  is certified as a lifeboat, its accommodation capacity may be included
  in the calculation under subsection (1) to reduce the number of life
  rafts required.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} The number of life rafts necessary to fulfil the
  requirements of subsection (1) shall be calculated in such a way that,
  taking into account the percentage of the complement that could be
  accommodated in each, should any one life raft be lost or rendered
  unserviceable, enough life rafts remain on each side of the ship to
  accommodate the complement.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-218, s. 34
:::

- **[[44]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-44 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  Class II ship of 500 tons gross tonnage or over shall carry, on each
  side of the ship, at least one rescue boat under launching devices.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} A Class II ship that is under 500 tons gross tonnage
  shall carry at least one rescue boat under launching devices.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-218, s. 34
:::

**[[45]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-45 .sectionLabel}** Every life raft on a
Class II ship shall be stowed

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} with its painter permanently attached to the ship;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} fitted with a float-free device;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} so that it can be launched by one or two persons; and

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} if it is inflatable, so that it inflates
  automatically if the ship sinks.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-218, s. 34
:::

**[[46]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-46 .sectionLabel}** A Class II ship shall
carry enough lifeboats and rescue boats to ensure that, in providing for
abandonment by the complement, each lifeboat or rescue boat need not
marshal more than nine life rafts.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-218, s. 34
:::

**[[47]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-47 .sectionLabel}** A Class II ship within
the length range set out in column I of an item of the table to this
section shall carry the supply of equipment set out in columns II to V
of that item.

- SOR/96-218, s. 34
:::

**[[48]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-48 .sectionLabel}** A Class II ship shall
carry

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} one lifejacket for each member of the complement; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the following supply of lifejackets:

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} enough conspicuously stowed on deck for 5 per cent
    of the complement,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} enough that are suitable for children for at least
    10 per cent of the complement or one for each child on board,
    whichever is greater,

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} enough for persons on watch, and

  - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} enough at locations that are remote from muster
    and embarkation stations for all of the persons likely to be there.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-218, s. 34
- SOR/2004-26, s. 15
:::

**[[49]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-49 .sectionLabel}** A Class II ship shall
carry

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} three survival craft VHF radiotelephone apparatus
  stowed so that they are readily accessible for immediate use, unless
  the ship is less than 20 m in length and engaged on a voyage that does
  not go beyond the Gulf of St. Lawrence;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} a marine anti-exposure work suit for each member of
  the rescue boat crew;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} for each life raft,

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} if the ship is engaged on a near coastal voyage,
    Class 1, limited to 120 nautical miles from shore, a SOLAS A pack as
    set out in the LSA Code, and

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} if the ship is engaged on a short international
    voyage that is not a sheltered waters voyage, a SOLAS B pack as set
    out in the LSA Code;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2023-257, s. 458\]]{.Repealed}

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2023-257, s. 458\]]{.Repealed}

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} a line-throwing appliance;

- [(g)]{.lawlabel} 12 rocket parachute flares;

- [(h)]{.lawlabel} one portable battery-powered loud hailer located at
  each muster station where

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} more than 100 persons are to be mustered, or

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} a marine evacuation system is used; and

- [(i)]{.lawlabel} means of embarkation into survival craft.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-218, s. 34
- SOR/2000-261, s. 15
- SOR/2001-179, ss. 27, 76(F)
- [SOR/2023-257, s. 458](#1431329-1435783){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1431329-1435783.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-49-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 49"){.PITLink}
:::

**[[50]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-50 .sectionLabel}** A Class II ship that is a
Safety Convention ship or is 20 m or over in length and engaged on a
voyage beyond the VHF coverage area or sea area A1 shall carry two SARTs
stowed so that they are readily accessible for immediate use and for
placing in the two survival craft that are launched first.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-218, s. 34
- SOR/2000-261, s. 16
:::

**[[51]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-51 .sectionLabel}** A Class II ship shall be
provided with signs that indicate

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the location of

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} survival craft and their launching devices,

  - [(i.1)]{.lawlabel} lifejackets and lifejackets suitable for
    children,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} muster stations, and

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} embarkation stations; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} directions to the muster and embarkation stations.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-218, s. 34
- SOR/2004-26, s. 16
:::

### [Class III Ships]{.HTitleText2}[(Ships that are over five tons gross tonnage, are not Safety Convention ships and are certified to carry more than 12 passengers on near coastal voyages, Class 2, or inland voyages)]{.HTitleText2} {#h-518388 .Subheading}

::: HistoricalNote
\[

- SOR/2023-257, s. 501

\]
:::

**[[52]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-52 .sectionLabel}** Subject to section 53, a
Class III ship shall carry on each side of the ship

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} enough life rafts to accommodate at least 60 per cent
  of the complement; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} one rescue boat under launching devices.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-218, s. 34
:::

**[[53]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-53 .sectionLabel}** A Class III ship that is
under 85 m in length may carry, instead of the survival craft referred
to in section 52,

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} on each side of the ship, enough life rafts to
  accommodate at least 50 per cent of the complement; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} at least one rescue boat under launching devices.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-218, s. 34
:::

**[[54]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-54 .sectionLabel}** A Class III ship shall
carry enough rescue boats to ensure that, in providing for abandonment
by the complement, each rescue boat need not marshal more than nine life
rafts.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-218, s. 34
:::

**[[55]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-55 .sectionLabel}** The requirements of
paragraphs 52(a) and 53(a) may be met by the carriage of lifeboats, or a
combination of lifeboats and life rafts, if the required survival craft
accommodation capacity is maintained.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-218, s. 34
:::

**[[56]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-56 .sectionLabel}** Every life raft on a
Class III ship shall be stowed

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} with its painter permanently attached to the ship;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} fitted with a float-free device;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} so that it can be launched by one or two persons; and

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} if it is inflatable, so that it inflates
  automatically if the ship sinks.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-218, s. 34
:::

- **[[57]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-57 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  Class III ship within the length range set out in column I of an item
  of the table to this subsection shall carry the supply of equipment
  set out in columns II to IV of that item.

  <figure id="518414">
  <table
  class="table table-hover table-striped tablePointsize9 topdouble topbot">
  <thead>
  <tr class="topdouble">
  <th class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom top"></th>
  <th class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom top" scope="col">Column I</th>

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Notwithstanding the requirements of subsection (1), a
  Class III ship shall carry at least one lifebuoy on each side of each
  passenger deck.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-218, s. 34
:::

**[[58]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-58 .sectionLabel}** A Class III ship shall
carry

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} one lifejacket for each member of the complement; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the following supply of lifejackets:

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} enough conspicuously stowed on deck for 5 per cent
    of the complement,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} enough that are suitable for children for at least
    10 per cent of the complement or one for each child on board,
    whichever is greater,

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} enough for persons on watch, and

  - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} enough at locations that are remote from muster
    and embarkation stations for all of the persons likely to be there.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-218, s. 34
- SOR/2004-26, s. 17
:::

**[[59]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-59 .sectionLabel}** A Class III ship shall
carry

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} a marine anti-exposure work suit for each member of
  the rescue boat crew;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} for each life raft, a SOLAS B pack as set out in the
  LSA Code;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2023-257, s. 459\]]{.Repealed}

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2023-257, s. 459\]]{.Repealed}

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} if the ship is 85 m or over in length, a
  line-throwing appliance;

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} 12 pyrotechnic distress signals, of which six are
  rocket parachute flares and six are rocket parachute or hand flares;

- [(g)]{.lawlabel} one portable battery-powered loud hailer located at
  each muster station where

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} more than 100 persons are to be mustered, or

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} a marine evacuation system is used;

- [(h)]{.lawlabel} means of embarkation into survival craft; and

- [(i)]{.lawlabel} if the ship is 20 m or over in length and engaged on
  a near coastal voyage, Class 2, three survival craft VHF
  radiotelephone apparatus stowed so that they are readily accessible
  for immediate use.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-218, s. 34
- SOR/2000-261, s. 17
- SOR/2001-179, ss. 28, 76(F)
- [SOR/2023-257, s. 459](#1431331-1435785){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1431331-1435785.html"
  role="button"}
- [SOR/2023-257, s. 502](#1431434-1436402){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1431434-1436402.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-59-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 59"){.PITLink}
:::

**[[60]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-60 .sectionLabel}** A Class III ship shall be
provided with signs that indicate

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the location of

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} survival craft and their launching devices,

  - [(i.1)]{.lawlabel} lifejackets and lifejackets suitable for
    children,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} muster stations, and

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} embarkation stations; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} directions to the muster and embarkation stations.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-218, s. 34
- SOR/2004-26, s. 18
:::

**[[60.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-60.1 .sectionLabel}** Instead of complying
with sections 52 to 60, a ship may comply with sections 61 to 65 if it

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} was a Class IV ship and a new ship on the day before
  the day on which subsection 148(4) comes into force;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} engages only on inland voyages, Class II, or minor
  waters voyages, Class I, or both, as those voyages are defined in
  subsections 5(2) and 6(1) of the [Home-Trade, Inland and Minor Waters
  Voyages Regulations](/eng/regulations/C.R.C.,_c._1430); and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} does not change its area of operation or equipment.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2023-257, s. 460](#1431333-1435797){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1431333-1435797.html"
  role="button"}
:::

### [Class IV Ships]{.HTitleText2}[(Ships that are over five tons gross tonnage, are not Safety Convention ships and are certified to carry more than 12 passengers on inland voyages, limited to 25 nautical miles from shore, or near coastal voyages, Class 2, limited to five nautical miles from shore)]{.HTitleText2} {#h-518455 .Subheading}

::: HistoricalNote
\[

- SOR/2023-257, s. 504

\]
:::

- **[[61]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-61 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  Class IV ship shall carry

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} enough life rafts to accommodate the complement;
    and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} one emergency boat.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The accommodation capacity of one emergency boat may
  be included in the calculation under paragraph (1)(a) to reduce the
  number of life rafts required.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} The requirements of subsection (1) may be met by the
  carriage of lifeboats, or a combination of lifeboats and life rafts,
  if the required survival craft accommodation capacity is maintained.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-218, s. 34
:::

- **[[62]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-62 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  Class IV ship within the length range set out in column I of an item
  of the table to this subsection shall carry the supply of equipment
  set out in columns II to IV of that item.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Notwithstanding the requirements of subsection (1), a
  Class IV ship shall carry at least one lifebuoy on each side of each
  passenger deck.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-218, s. 34
:::

**[[63]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-63 .sectionLabel}** A Class IV ship shall
carry

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} one lifejacket for each member of the complement; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} enough lifejackets that are suitable for children for
  at least 10 per cent of the complement or one for each child on board,
  whichever is greater.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-218, s. 34
- SOR/2004-26, s. 19
:::

**[[64]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-64 .sectionLabel}** A Class IV ship shall
carry

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} for each life raft, a SOLAS B pack as set out in the
  LSA Code;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2023-257, s. 461\]]{.Repealed}

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2023-257, s. 461\]]{.Repealed}

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} 12 pyrotechnic distress signals, of which six are
  rocket parachute flares;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} one portable battery-powered loud hailer located at
  each muster station where

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} more than 100 persons are to be mustered, or

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} a marine evacuation system is used; and

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} means of embarkation into survival craft.

::: HistoricalNote

**[[65]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-65 .sectionLabel}** A Class IV ship shall be
provided with signs that indicate

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the location of

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} survival craft and their launching devices,

  - [(i.1)]{.lawlabel} lifejackets and lifejackets suitable for
    children,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} muster stations, and

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} embarkation stations; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} directions to the muster and embarkation stations.

::: HistoricalNote

- SOR/2004-26, s. 20
:::

**[[65.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-65.1 .sectionLabel}** Instead of complying
with sections 61 to 65, a ship may comply with sections 66 to 71 if it

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} was a Class V ship and a new ship on the day before
  the day on which subsection 148(4) comes into force;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} engages only on home-trade voyages, Class IV, or
  minor waters voyages, Class II, or both, as those voyages are defined
  in subsections 4(4) and 6(2) of the [Home-Trade, Inland and Minor
  Waters Voyages Regulations](/eng/regulations/C.R.C.,_c._1430); and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} does not change its area of operation or equipment.

### [Class V Ships]{.HTitleText2}[(Ships that are over five tons gross tonnage, are not Safety Convention ships and are certified to carry more than 12 passengers on sheltered waters voyages)]{.HTitleText2} {#h-518500 .Subheading}

::: HistoricalNote
\[

- SOR/2023-257, s. 500

\]
:::

- **[[66]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-66 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  Class V ship shall carry

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} enough life rafts or inflatable rescue platforms to
    accommodate the complement; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} unless the ship has a freeboard of less than 1.5 m
    or is fitted with a boarding platform, one emergency boat.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} If a ship navigates in waters the temperature of
  which is 15°C or more, the requirement in respect of the accommodation
  capacity of the life rafts or inflatable rescue platforms that is
  referred to in paragraph (1)(a) or 67(b) may be met by counting not
  more than 33.33 per cent of the complement of the life raft or
  inflatable rescue platform as being in the water, holding on to the
  life raft or inflatable rescue platform.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-218, s. 34
- SOR/2006-256, s. 4
- SOR/2013-235, s. 4
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-66-20061019.html "Link to previous version of section 66"){.PITLink}
:::

**[[67]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-67 .sectionLabel}** A Class V ship that
navigates in waters the temperature of which is 15°C or more may carry,
instead of the survival craft referred to in subsection 66(1),

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} one lifebuoy for every four members of the complement
  if the ship navigates

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} within 150 m of shore, or

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} in a depth of water not exceeding 1.5 m; or

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} enough buoyant apparatus to accommodate not more than
  40 per cent of the complement of the ship and enough life rafts or
  inflatable rescue platforms to accommodate that portion of the
  complement not accommodated by the buoyant apparatus.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-218, s. 34
- SOR/2001-179, s. 30
- SOR/2006-256, s. 5
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-67-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 67"){.PITLink}
:::

- **[[68]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-68 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  Class V ship within the length range set out in column I of an item of
  the table to this subsection shall carry the supply of equipment set
  out in columns II to IV of that item.

carry

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} one lifejacket for each member of the complement; and

  at least 10 per cent of the complement or one for each child on board,
  whichever is greater.

:::

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2023-257, s. 463\]]{.Repealed}
