### [Class VI Ships]{.HTitleText2}[(Ships that are not over five tons gross tonnage and that are certified to carry more than 12 passengers)]{.HTitleText2} {#h-518550 .Subheading}

- **[[72]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-72 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  Class VI ship shall carry

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} where the ship is engaged on a sheltered waters
    voyage, enough life rafts or inflatable rescue platforms to
    accommodate the complement; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} where the ship is engaged on any other voyage,
    enough life rafts to accommodate the complement.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} If a ship navigates in waters the temperature of
  which is 15°C or more, the requirement in respect of the accommodation
  capacity of the life rafts or inflatable rescue platforms that is
  referred to in paragraph (1)(a) or 3(b) may be met by counting not
  more than 33.33 per cent of the complement of the life raft or
  inflatable rescue platform as being in the water, holding on to the
  life raft or inflatable rescue platform.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} A Class VI ship that navigates in waters the
  temperature of which is 15°C or more may carry, instead of the
  survival craft referred to in paragraph (1)(a),

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} one lifebuoy for every four members of the
    complement if the ship operates

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} within 150 m of shore, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} in a depth of water not exceeding 1.5 m; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} enough buoyant apparatus to accommodate not more
    than 40 per cent of the complement of the ship and enough life rafts
    or inflatable rescue platforms to accommodate that portion of the
    complement not accommodated by the buoyant apparatus.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-218, s. 34
- SOR/2006-256, s. 6
- SOR/2013-235, s. 5
- [SOR/2023-257, s. 505](#1431444-1435877){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1431444-1435877.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-72-20131206.html "Link to previous version of section 72"){.PITLink}
:::

**[[73]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-73 .sectionLabel}** A Class VI ship shall
carry

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} one lifejacket for each member of the complement; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} enough lifejackets that are suitable for children for
  at least 10 per cent of the complement or one for each child on board,
  whichever is greater.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-218, s. 34
- SOR/2004-26, s. 23
:::

**[[74]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-74 .sectionLabel}** A Class VI ship that
carries survival craft shall carry

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} for each life raft and each inflatable rescue
  platform,

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} where the ship is engaged on a voyage beyond the
    limits of a near coastal voyage, Class 2, a SOLAS A pack as set out
    in the LSA Code, or

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} in any other case, a SOLAS B pack as set out in
    the LSA Code;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} six pyrotechnic distress signals, of which three are
  rocket parachute flares; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} at least one lifebuoy on each side of each passenger
  deck, of which one on each deck is fitted with a buoyant lifeline.

- [(d) and (e)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2001-179, s.
  32\]]{.Repealed}

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-218, s. 34
- SOR/2001-179, s. 32
- [SOR/2023-257, s. 464](#1431341-1435840){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1431341-1435840.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-74-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 74"){.PITLink}
:::

**[[75]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-75 .sectionLabel}** A Class VI ship shall be
provided with signs that indicate the location of life saving equipment
that is not stowed in plain view.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-218, s. 34
- SOR/2001-179, s. 33
:::

### [Class VII Ships]{.HTitleText2}[(Ships that are over five tons gross tonnage, are certified to carry passengers, are not self-propelled and are towed or pushed by a ship or operated by a cable)]{.HTitleText2} {#h-518581 .Subheading}

- **[[76]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-76 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  Class VII ship shall carry

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} where the ship is engaged on a sheltered waters
    voyage, enough life rafts or inflatable rescue platforms to
    accommodate the complement; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} where the ship is engaged on any other voyage,
    enough life rafts to accommodate the complement.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} If a ship navigates in waters the temperature of
  which is 15°C or more, the requirement in respect of the accommodation
  capacity referred to in paragraph (1)(a) or (3)(b) may be met by
  counting not more than 33.33 per cent of the complement of the life
  raft or inflatable rescue platform as being in the water, holding on
  to the life raft or inflatable rescue platform.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} A Class VII ship that navigates in waters the
  temperature of which is 15°C or more may carry, instead of the
  survival craft referred to in paragraph (1)(a),

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} one lifebuoy for every four members of the
    complement if the ship navigates

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} within 150 m of shore, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} in a depth of water not exceeding 1.5 m; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} enough buoyant apparatus to accommodate not more
    than 40 per cent of the complement of the ship and enough life rafts
    or inflatable rescue platforms to accommodate that portion of the
    complement not accommodated by the buoyant apparatus.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} A Class VII ship shall have an emergency boat that is
  carried on board or towed.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-218, s. 34
- SOR/2001-179, s. 34
- SOR/2006-256, s. 7
- [SOR/2023-257, s. 505](#1431444-1435878){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1431444-1435878.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-76-20061019.html "Link to previous version of section 76"){.PITLink}
:::

**[[77]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-77 .sectionLabel}** A Class VII ship within
the length range set out in column I of an item of the table to this
section shall carry the supply of equipment set out in columns II and
III of that item.

**[[78]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-78 .sectionLabel}** A Class VII ship shall
carry

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} one lifejacket for each member of the complement; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} enough lifejackets that are suitable for children for
  at least 10 per cent of the complement or one for each child on board,
  whichever is greater.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-218, s. 34
- SOR/2004-26, s. 24
:::

**[[79]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-79 .sectionLabel}** A Class VII ship shall
carry

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} for each life raft and each inflatable rescue
  platform,

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} where the ship is engaged on a voyage beyond the
    limits of a near coastal voyage, Class 2, a SOLAS A pack as set out
    in the LSA Code, or

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} in any other case, a SOLAS B pack as set out in
    the LSA Code;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2023-257, s. 465\]]{.Repealed}

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} six hand flares;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} one portable battery-powered loud hailer located at
  each muster station where

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} more than 100 persons are to be mustered, or

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} a marine evacuation system is used; and

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} means of embarkation into survival craft.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-218, s. 34
- SOR/2001-179, ss. 35, 76(F)
- [SOR/2023-257, s. 465](#1431343-1435844){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1431343-1435844.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-79-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 79"){.PITLink}
:::

**[[80]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-80 .sectionLabel}** A Class VII ship shall be
provided with signs that indicate

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the location of

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} survival craft and their launching devices,

  - [(i.1)]{.lawlabel} lifejackets and lifejackets suitable for
    children,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} muster stations, and

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} embarkation stations; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} directions to the muster and embarkation stations.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-218, s. 34
- SOR/2004-26, s. 25
:::

### [Class VIII Ships \[Reserved\]]{.HTitleText2} {#h-518631 .Subheading}

::: HistoricalNote
\[

- SOR/96-218, s. 34

\]
:::

### [Class IX Ships]{.HTitleText2}[(Ships that are over 15 tons gross tonnage and are (i) Safety Convention ships that are not certified to carry passengers, or that are certified to carry 12 or fewer passengers, on unlimited voyages, or (ii) ships that are not Safety Convention ships and that are not certified to carry passengers, or that are certified to carry 12 or fewer passengers, on unlimited voyages or near coastal voyages, Class 1)]{.HTitleText2} {#h-518634 .Subheading}

::: HistoricalNote
\[

- SOR/2023-257, s. 466

\]
:::

- **[[81]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-81
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to sections 82, 83 and 85, a
  Class IX ship shall carry, on each side of the ship, enough totally
  enclosed lifeboats under gravity-type davits to accommodate the
  complement.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} In addition to meeting the requirements of subsection
  (1), a Class IX ship shall carry on each side of the ship, enough life
  rafts to accommodate

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the complement, if the life rafts are stowed in a
    location providing for easy side-to-side transfer at a single
    open-deck level; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} 150 per cent of the complement, if the life rafts
    are not stowed in accordance with paragraph (a).

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-218, s. 34
:::

**[[82]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-82 .sectionLabel}** A Class IX ship that has
free-fall launching devices may carry, instead of the lifeboats referred
to in subsection 81(1), enough totally enclosed lifeboats capable of
being free-fall launched over the stern of the ship to accommodate the
complement.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-218, s. 34
:::

- **[[83]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-83
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), a Class
  IX ship that is 85 m or under in length, other than a tanker, may
  carry on each side of the ship, instead of the survival craft referred
  to in section 81, enough life rafts to accommodate

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the complement, if the life rafts are stowed in a
    location providing for easy side-to-side transfer at a single
    open-deck level; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} 150 per cent of the complement, if the life rafts
    are not stowed in accordance with paragraph (a).

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The number of life rafts necessary to fulfil the
  requirements of subsection (1) shall be calculated in such a way that,
  taking into account the percentage of the complement that could be
  accommodated in each, should any one life raft be lost or rendered
  unserviceable, enough life rafts remain on each side of the ship to
  accommodate the complement.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-218, s. 34
:::

**[[84]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-84 .sectionLabel}** Where the Class IX ship
referred to in subsection 81(1) or section 82 is a tanker, the lifeboats
shall be fire-protected and shall have a self-contained air support
system.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-218, s. 34
:::

**[[85]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-85 .sectionLabel}** A Class IX ship that is
not a tanker and is a Safety Convention ship engaged on a sheltered
waters voyage, may carry partially enclosed lifeboats instead of totally
enclosed lifeboats.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-218, s. 34
- [SOR/2023-257, s. 505](#1431444-1436484){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1431444-1436484.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-85-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 85"){.PITLink}
:::

**[[86]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-86 .sectionLabel}** Where the survival craft
in a Class IX ship are stowed more than 100 m from the stem or stern,
the ship shall carry an additional life raft stowed as far forward or as
far aft as is practicable, as the case may be.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-218, s. 34
:::

**[[87]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-87 .sectionLabel}** A Class IX ship shall
carry at least one rescue boat under launching devices, unless one of
the ship's lifeboats meets the requirements for a rescue boat.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-218, s. 34
:::

**[[88]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-88 .sectionLabel}** Every life raft on a
Class IX ship, other than a life raft referred to in section 86, shall
be stowed

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} with its painter permanently attached to the ship;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} fitted with a float-free device; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} if it is inflatable, so that it inflates
  automatically if the ship sinks.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-218, s. 34
:::

**[[89]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-89 .sectionLabel}** A Class IX ship within
the length range set out in column I of an item of the table to this
section shall carry the supply of equipment set out in columns II to V
of that item.

**[[90]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-90 .sectionLabel}** A Class IX ship shall
carry

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} one lifejacket, fitted with a whistle and a personal
  locator light, for each member of the complement; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the following supply of lifejackets, each fitted with
  a whistle and a personal locator light:

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} enough that are suitable for children, for all of
    the children on board,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} enough for all of the persons on watch, of which
    at least two are stowed in the wheelhouse and two in the engine
    room, and

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} enough at locations that are remote from
    embarkation stations for all of the persons likely to be there.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-218, s. 34
:::

**[[91]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-91 .sectionLabel}** A Class IX ship shall
carry

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the following number of survival craft VHF
  radiotelephone apparatus stowed so that they are readily accessible
  for immediate use:

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} two, if the ship is 300 tons or over but under 500
    tons gross tonnage, and

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} three, if the ship is 500 tons gross tonnage or
    over;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} for each life raft, a SOLAS A pack as set out in the
  LSA Code;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2023-257, s. 467\]]{.Repealed}

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2023-257, s. 467\]]{.Repealed}

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} a line-throwing appliance;

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} 12 rocket parachute flares;

- [(g)]{.lawlabel} one immersion suit, fitted with a whistle and a
  personal locator light, for each member of the complement; and

- [(h)]{.lawlabel} means of embarkation into survival craft.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-218, s. 34
- SOR/2000-261, s. 18
- SOR/2001-179, s. 36
- [SOR/2023-257, s. 467](#1431347-1436429){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1431347-1436429.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-91-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 91"){.PITLink}
:::

- **[[92]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-92 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  Class IX ship shall carry the following SARTs:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} if the ship is 20 m or over in length but under 500
    tons gross tonnage, one SART stowed so that it is readily accessible
    for immediate use and for placing in one of the survival craft that
    are launched first; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if the ship is 500 tons gross tonnage or over, two
    SARTs stowed so that they are readily accessible for immediate use
    and for placing in the two survival craft that are launched first.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Despite subsection (1), a ship that is under 300 tons
  gross tonnage and that on March 31, 2001 was required by these
  Regulations to carry two Class II EPIRBs may continue to carry them
  instead of a SART until one of the batteries of the Class II EPIRBs
  needs to be replaced.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-218, s. 34
- SOR/2000-261, s. 19
:::

**[[93]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-93 .sectionLabel}** A Class IX ship shall be
provided with signs that indicate

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the location of

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} survival craft and their launching devices, and

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} embarkation stations; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} directions to the embarkation stations.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-218, s. 34
:::

### [Class X Ships]{.HTitleText2}[(Ships that are over 15 tons gross tonnage, are not Safety Convention ships and are not certified to carry passengers, or are certified to carry 12 or fewer passengers, on near coastal voyages, Class 1, limited to 120 nautical miles from shore, near coastal voyages, Class 2, sheltered waters voyages, inland voyages, inland voyages, limited to 25 nautical miles from shore, or near coastal voyages, Class 2, limited to five nautical miles from shore)]{.HTitleText2} {#h-518714 .Subheading}

::: HistoricalNote
\[

- SOR/2023-257, s. 468

\]
:::

**[[94]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-94 .sectionLabel}** A Class X ship that is
not a tanker shall carry, on each side of the ship, enough life rafts to
accommodate the complement.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-218, s. 34
:::

- **[[95]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-95 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  Class X ship that is 85 m or under in length and is not a tanker shall
  carry

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} if the ship is engaged on a near coastal voyage,
    Class 1, limited to 120 nautical miles from shore, or an inland
    voyage, one rescue boat under a launching device; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in any other case, one emergency boat under a
    launching device.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} A Class X ship that is over 85 m in length and is not
  a tanker shall carry

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} if the ship is engaged on a near coastal voyage,
    Class 1, limited to 120 nautical miles from shore, one rescue boat
    under a launching device on each side of the ship;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if the ship is engaged on a near coastal voyage,
    Class 2, or an inland voyage, one rescue boat under a launching
    device; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} if the ship is engaged on any other voyage, one
    emergency boat under a launching device.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-218, s. 34
- SOR/2006-256, s. 8
- SOR/2013-235, s. 6
- [SOR/2023-257, s. 469](#1431351-1435858){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1431351-1435858.html"
  role="button"}
- [SOR/2023-257, s. 508](#1431463-1436386){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1431463-1436386.html"
  role="button"}
- [SOR/2023-257, s. 511](#1431474-1436398){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1431474-1436398.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-95-20131206.html "Link to previous version of section 95"){.PITLink}
:::

- **[[96]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-96
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to section 97, a Class X
  ship that is a tanker shall carry, on each side of the ship, enough
  totally enclosed lifeboats under launching devices to accommodate the
  complement.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The lifeboats referred to in subsection (1) shall be
  fire-protected and have a self-contained air support system, unless
  the ship is engaged

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} on a sheltered waters voyage; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} solely in the carriage of bunker oils and marine
    diesel oils, the flashpoint of which exceeds 60°C, as determined in
    a closed-cup test.

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2023-257, s. 470\]]{.Repealed}

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} In addition to meeting the requirements of subsection
  (1), a Class X ship that is a tanker shall carry, on each side of the
  ship, enough life rafts to accommodate

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the complement, if the life rafts are stowed in a
    location providing for easy side-to-side transfer at a single
    open-deck level; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} 150 per cent of the complement, if the life rafts
    are not stowed in accordance with paragraph (a).

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-218, s. 34
- [SOR/2023-257, s. 470](#1431353-1435860){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1431353-1435860.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-96-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 96"){.PITLink}
:::

- **[[97]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-97 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  Class X ship that is a tanker and has free-fall launching devices may
  carry, instead of the lifeboats required by subsection 96(1), enough
  totally enclosed lifeboats, capable of being free-fall launched over
  the stern of the ship, to accommodate the complement.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The lifeboats referred to in subsection (1) shall be
  fire-protected and shall have a self-contained air support system
  unless the ship is engaged

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} on a sheltered waters voyage; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} solely in the carriage of bunker oils and marine
    diesel oils, the flashpoint of which exceeds 60°C, as determined in
    a closed-cup test.

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2023-257, s. 471\]]{.Repealed}

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-218, s. 34
- SOR/2004-253, s. 2
- [SOR/2023-257, s. 471](#1431355-1435864){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1431355-1435864.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-97-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 97"){.PITLink}
:::

**[[98]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-98 .sectionLabel}** Lifeboats carried on a
Class X ship that is not a tanker shall be

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} where the ship is engaged on a near coastal voyage,
  Class 1, limited to 120 nautical miles from shore, totally enclosed;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} where the ship is engaged on a near coastal voyage,
  Class 2, or an inland voyage, partially enclosed self-righting or
  totally enclosed; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} where the ship is engaged on an inland voyage,
  limited to 25 nautical miles from shore, or a near coastal voyage,
  Class 2, limited to five nautical miles from shore, partially or
  totally enclosed.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-218, s. 34
- [SOR/2023-257, s. 508](#1431463-1436389){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1431463-1436389.html"
  role="button"}
- [SOR/2023-257, s. 509](#1431467-1436390){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1431467-1436390.html"
  role="button"}
- [SOR/2023-257, s. 511](#1431474-1436400){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1431474-1436400.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-98-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 98"){.PITLink}
:::

- **[[99]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-99 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  Class X ship that is over 100 m in length and carries its survival
  craft aft shall carry enough life rafts stowed in the forward part of
  the ship to accommodate all of the persons who are berthed there.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where the survival craft in a Class X ship are stowed
  more than 100 m from the stem or stern, the ship shall carry a life
  raft stowed as far forward or as far aft as is practicable, as the
  case may be.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-218, s. 34
:::

**[[100]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-100 .sectionLabel}** A Class X ship within
the length range set out in column I of an item of the table to this
section shall carry the supply of equipment set out in columns II to IV
of that item.

<figcaption><p>TABLE</p></figcaption>
</figure>

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-218, s. 34
:::

**[[101]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-101 .sectionLabel}** A Class X ship shall
carry

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} one lifejacket, fitted with a whistle and a personal
  locator light, for each member of the complement; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the following supply of lifejackets, each fitted with
  a whistle and a personal locator light:

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} at least two stowed in the wheelhouse,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} at least two stowed in the engine room, and

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} enough that are suitable for children for all of
    the children on board.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-218, s. 34
:::

**[[102]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-102 .sectionLabel}** A Class X ship shall
carry

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the following number of survival craft VHF
  radiotelephone apparatus stowed so that they are readily accessible
  for immediate use:

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} two, in the case of a ship that is 300 tons or over
    but under 500 tons gross tonnage and is engaged on a near coastal
    voyage, Class 1, limited to 120 nautical miles from shore, or a near
    coastal voyage, Class 2, and

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} three, in the case of a ship that is 500 tons
    gross tonnage or over and is engaged on a near coastal voyage, Class
    1, limited to 120 nautical miles from shore, or a near coastal
    voyage, Class 2;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} where the ship is engaged on a voyage other than a
  sheltered waters voyage, one immersion suit, fitted with a whistle and
  a personal locator light, for each member of the complement;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} for each life raft,

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} if the ship is engaged on a near coastal voyage,
    Class 1, limited to 120 nautical miles from shore, a SOLAS A pack as
    set out in the LSA Code, or

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} in any other case, a SOLAS B pack as set out in
    the LSA Code;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2023-257, s. 472\]]{.Repealed}

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2023-257, s. 472\]]{.Repealed}

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2023-257, s. 472\]]{.Repealed}

- [(g)]{.lawlabel} if the ship is 85 m in length or over and is engaged
  on a voyage other than a sheltered waters voyage, a line-throwing
  appliance;

- [(h)]{.lawlabel} the following pyrotechnic distress signals:

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} where the ship is under 85 m in length, 12
    pyrotechnic distress signals of which six are rocket parachute
    flares, and

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} where the ship is 85 m in length or over, 12
    rocket parachute flares;

- [(i)]{.lawlabel} means of embarkation into survival craft; and

- [(j)]{.lawlabel} the following SARTs:

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a ship that is 300 tons or over but
    under 500 tons gross tonnage and is engaged on a voyage beyond the
    VHF coverage area or sea area A1, one SART stowed so that it is
    readily accessible for immediate use and for placing in one of the
    survival craft that are launched first, and

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a ship that is 500 tons gross
    tonnage or over and is engaged on a voyage beyond the VHF coverage
    area or sea area A1, two SARTs stowed so that they are readily
    accessible for immediate use and for placing in the two survival
    craft that are launched first.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-218, s. 34
- SOR/2000-261, s. 20
- SOR/2001-179, s. 37
- [SOR/2023-257, s. 472](#1431357-1435868){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1431357-1435868.html"
  role="button"}
- [SOR/2023-257, s. 505](#1431444-1436457){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1431444-1436457.html"
  role="button"}
- [SOR/2023-257, s. 510](#1431470-1436458){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1431470-1436458.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-102-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 102"){.PITLink}
:::

**[[103]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-103 .sectionLabel}** A Class X ship shall be
provided with signs that indicate

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the location of

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} survival craft and their launching devices, and

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} embarkation stations; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} directions to the embarkation stations.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-218, s. 34
:::

### [Class XI Ships]{.HTitleText2}[(Ships that are over 15 tons gross tonnage, are not certified to carry passengers but carry a crew, are not self-propelled and are towed or pushed by a ship or operated by a cable)]{.HTitleText2} {#h-518804 .Subheading}

- **[[104]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-104 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  Class XI ship that is 85 m in length or over and is engaged on a
  voyage more than 20 nautical miles from shore shall carry

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the following lifeboats and life rafts:

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} on each side of the ship, enough partially
      enclosed lifeboats under launching devices to accommodate the
      complement, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} enough life rafts, but no fewer than two, to
      accommodate the complement; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the following life rafts and rescue boats:

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} on each side of the ship, enough life rafts to
      accommodate the complement,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} enough life rafts, but no fewer than two, to
      accommodate the complement, and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} at least one rescue boat with a means of
      launching.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} In addition to meeting the requirements of subsection
  (1), a Class XI ship that carries its survival craft more than 100 m
  from an area where persons are berthed shall carry, readily available
  to the persons berthed there, enough life rafts to accommodate them.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-218, s. 34
:::

**[[105]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-105 .sectionLabel}** A Class XI ship that is
under 85 m in length and is engaged on a voyage more than 20 nautical
miles from shore shall carry

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} on each side of the ship, enough life rafts to
  accommodate the complement; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} one emergency boat with a means of launching.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-218, s. 34
:::

- **[[106]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-106 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  Class XI ship that is engaged on a voyage not more than 20 nautical
  miles from shore shall carry

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} enough life rafts to accommodate the complement;
    and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} one emergency boat with a means of launching.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} In addition to meeting the requirements of subsection
  (1), a Class XI ship that carries its survival craft more than 100 m
  from an area where persons are berthed shall carry, readily available
  to the persons berthed there, enough life rafts to accommodate them.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-218, s. 34
:::

- **[[107]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-107 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  Class XI ship within the length range set out in column I of an item
  of the table to this section shall carry the supply of equipment set
  out in columns II to IV of that item.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Notwithstanding the requirements of subsection (1), a
  Class XI ship need not carry more lifebuoys than there are members of
  the complement.

**[[108]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-108 .sectionLabel}** A Class XI ship shall
carry

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} one lifejacket, fitted with a whistle and a personal
  locator light, for each member of the complement;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} where the ship is engaged on a voyage other than a
  sheltered waters voyage, one immersion suit, fitted with a whistle and
  a personal locator light, for each member of the complement;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} for each life raft,

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} if the ship is engaged on a near coastal voyage,
    Class 1, or a near coastal voyage, Class 1 that is limited to 120
    nautical miles from shore, a SOLAS A pack as set out in the LSA
    Code, or

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} in any other case, a SOLAS B pack as set out in
    the LSA Code;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} [ \[Repealed, SOR/2023-257, s. 473\]]{.Repealed}

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} [ \[Repealed, SOR/2023-257, s. 473\]]{.Repealed}

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2023-257, s. 473\]]{.Repealed}

- [(g)]{.lawlabel} one line-throwing appliance, unless the ship

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} is engaged on a sheltered waters voyage, or

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} is making a voyage solely under tow and the tow
    boat is equipped with a line-throwing appliance;

- [(h)]{.lawlabel} six rocket parachute flares; and

- [(i)]{.lawlabel} means of embarkation into survival craft.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-218, s. 34
- SOR/2001-179, s. 38

**[[109]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-109 .sectionLabel}** A Class XI ship shall
be provided with signs that indicate

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the location of

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} survival craft and their launching devices, and

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} embarkation stations; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} directions to the embarkation stations.

::: HistoricalNote

## [PART III]{.HLabel1}[Operational Requirements and Equipment Standards]{.HTitleText1} {#h-518864 .Part}

### [Life Saving Equipment Plans]{.HTitleText2} {#h-518865 .Subheading}

**[[110]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-110 .sectionLabel}** [\[Repealed,
SOR/2023-257, s. 474\]]{.Repealed}

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-110-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 110"){.PITLink}
:::

- **[[110.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-110.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every ship shall carry, prominently
  displayed, the most recent life saving equipment plan that the Board
  approved for the ship.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Despite subsection (1), a passenger ship of 25 m in
  length or under is not required to display a life saving equipment
  plan if doing so is impracticable because of the size or design of the
  ship.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} Every passenger ship shall make an announcement for
  the information of passengers before the ship leaves any place where
  passengers embark.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} The announcement shall

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} specify the location of lifejackets;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in each area of the ship, inform the passengers in
    that area of the location of lifejackets that are closest to them;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} specify the location of survival craft and muster
    stations; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} in each area of the ship, inform the passengers in
    that area of the location of survival craft that are closest to
    them.

- [(5)]{.lawlabel} The announcement shall be

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in either official language or in both, according
    to the needs of the passengers; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the most recent format approved by the Board as
    meeting the requirements of this section.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2001-179, s. 40
- SOR/2002-122, s. 2
:::

### [Evacuation Procedures]{.HTitleText2} {#h-518893 .Subheading}

**[[111]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-111 .sectionLabel}** Every passenger ship
shall have an evacuation procedure for the safe evacuation of the
complement from the ship within 30 minutes after the abandon-ship signal
is given.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-218, s. 34
- SOR/2006-256, s. 9
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-111-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 111"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Evacuation of New Ships, Class IX]{.HTitleText2} {#h-518897 .Subheading}

**[[112]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-112 .sectionLabel}** The survival craft
required for a new ship that is a Class IX ship shall be capable of
being launched with their full complement and equipment within 10
minutes after the abandon-ship signal is given.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-218, s. 34
:::

### [Maintenance, Servicing and Training]{.HTitleText2} {#h-518901 .Subheading}

**[[113]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-113 .sectionLabel}** The following
inspections and tests shall be carried out and recorded in the official
log of a ship once every week on a Safety Convention ship and once every
two weeks on any other ship:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} survival craft and launching devices shall be
  visually inspected to ensure that they are ready for use;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the motor of lifeboats and rescue boats shall be run
  ahead and astern for a total period of not less than three minutes;
  and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the general emergency alarm system shall be tested.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-218, s.34
- SOR/2001-179, s. 41
- SOR/2004-26, s. 26
:::

- **[[114]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-114
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every ship shall carry maintenance
  manuals for life saving equipment that contain the following
  information from the equipment manufacturers:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} maintenance and repair instructions;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} schedules for periodic maintenance;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} diagrams of lubrication points with recommended
    lubricants;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} lists of replaceable parts;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} if available, up-to-date lists of suppliers of
    spare parts; and

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} logs for the records of inspection and maintenance.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The maintenance manuals shall be

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} drafted in easily understood terms; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} made available

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} in English and French, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} in sufficient numbers to provide easy access to
      all crew members.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} Maintenance of life saving equipment shall be carried
  out in accordance with International Maritime Organization Resolution
  MSC.402(96), Requirements for maintenance, thorough examination,
  operational testing, overhaul and repair of lifeboats and rescue
  boats, launching appliances and release gear, as amended from time to
  time, and the instructions in the manuals referred to in subsection
  (1).

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} Despite subsection (3), emergency repairs to
  emergency boats and rescue boats may be carried out on board ship if
  permanent repairs are then carried out at a service station.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-218, s. 34
- SOR/2001-179, s. 42
- [SOR/2023-257, s. 475](#1431362-1435925){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1431362-1435925.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-114-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 114"){.PITLink}
:::

**[[115]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-115 .sectionLabel}** Every ship shall carry
spare parts and repair equipment for the life saving equipment and
components that need regular replacement.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-218, s. 34
:::

- **[[116]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-116
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where a ship carries survival craft
  that use falls as a means of launching, the falls shall be inspected
  in accordance with the appendix to annex 1 of International Maritime
  Organization Circular MSC.1/Circ.1206/Rev.1, Measures to prevent
  accidents with lifeboats, as amended from time to time, with special
  regard for areas passing through sheaves, and replaced when necessary
  due to deterioration of the falls or at intervals of not more than
  five years, whichever is earlier.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} For the purpose of interpreting the appendix referred
  to in subsection (1), "should" is to be read as "must" and any
  recommendation or suggestion is to be read as an obligation.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-218, s. 34
- [SOR/2023-257, s. 476](#1431364-1435927){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1431364-1435927.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-116-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 116"){.PITLink}
:::

- **[[117]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-117
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every ship shall carry training
  manuals that explain how to use the ship's life saving equipment.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The training manuals shall be

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} drafted in easily understood terms; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} made available

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} in English and French, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} in sufficient numbers to provide easy access to
      all crew members.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-218, s. 34
:::

- **[[118]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-118 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  owner of a service station that services inflatable survival equipment
  shall ensure that the equipment is serviced in accordance with the
  annex to International Maritime Organization Resolution A.761(18),
  Recommendation on conditions for the approval of servicing stations
  for inflatable liferafts, as amended from time to time.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} For the purpose of interpreting the annex referred to
  in subsection (1), "should" is to be read as "must" and any
  recommendation or suggestion is to be read as an obligation.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-218, s. 34
- SOR/2001-179, s. 43
- [SOR/2023-257, s. 477](#1431366-1435931){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1431366-1435931.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-118-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 118"){.PITLink}
:::

- **[[119]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-119 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  authorized representative of a ship shall ensure that the ship's
  inflatable survival equipment is serviced annually.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Despite subsection (1), the interval between
  servicing may be two years if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the ship on which the inflatable survival equipment
    is carried

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} is not a Safety Convention ship,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} operates for less than seven months per year,
      and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} operates only during months in which the
      monthly historical average daily minimum air temperature is
      greater than 0°C according to the climate data compiled by the
      Department of the Environment from the weather station nearest to
      the ship's area of operation;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} fewer than 15 years have elapsed since the day on
    which the inflatable survival equipment was manufactured;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the validity period of the most recent hydrostatic
    test of the gas cylinders of the inflatable survival equipment will
    not expire before the next servicing; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the inflatable survival equipment is stored in a
    dry location during the months in which the ship is not in
    operation.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} Despite subsection (1), the interval between
  servicing may be 30 months for inflatable survival equipment that is
  type approved in accordance with International Maritime Organization
  Circular MSC.1/Circ.1328, Guidelines for the Approval of Inflatable
  Liferafts Subject to Extended Service Intervals Not Exceeding 30
  Months, as amended from time to time.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of subsection (3), on a ship that is
  not a Safety Convention ship, the annual onboard inspection by
  inspection personnel that is required in the circular may be performed
  by a qualified crew member if the equipment is provided with
  manufacturer guidance on how to perform the inspection.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-218, s. 34
- SOR/2001-179, s. 44
- [SOR/2023-257, s. 477](#1431366-1436473){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1431366-1436473.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-119-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 119"){.PITLink}
:::

**[[120]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-120 .sectionLabel}** [\[Repealed,
SOR/2023-257, s. 486\]]{.Repealed}

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-120-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 120"){.PITLink}

### [Equipment Requiring Board Approval]{.HTitleText2} {#h-518962 .Subheading}

- **[[121]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-121 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Life
  saving equipment that is carried on a ship and is set out in column I
  of an item of the table to this section shall

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} meet the requirements set out in the Schedule to
    these Regulations or the Standard, as amended from time to time, set
    out in column II of that item; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} be approved as having met the requirements referred
    to in paragraph (a).

    <td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom top">Lifejackets (ships that are

    lifejacket;</p></li>
    <li><p><span class="tablelabel">(2)</span> Canadian General Standards
    Board standard CAN/CGSB-65.16-2005, Immersion Suit Systems; or</p></li>
    <li><p><span class="tablelabel">(3)</span> Underwriters Laboratories
    standards ANSI/CAN/UL 15027-2, Standard for Immersion Suits - Part 2:

  45\]]{.Repealed}

### [Buoyant Apparatus]{.HTitleText2} {#h-518976 .Subheading}

- **[[122]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-122
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every buoyant apparatus carried on a
  ship shall bear a label of the United States Coast Guard indicating
  that the apparatus meets the requirements of subpart 160.010 of Title
  46, Volume 6, of the Code of Federal Regulations of the United States.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Every marking on a buoyant apparatus carried on a
  ship shall be in English and French. This requirement does not apply
  in respect of the label required by subsection (1).

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-218, s. 34
- SOR/2001-179, s. 46
- SOR/2006-256, s. 11
- SOR/2013-235, s. 7
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-122-20061019.html "Link to previous version of section 122"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Pyrotechnic Distress Signals]{.HTitleText2} {#h-518982 .Subheading}

**[[123]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-123 .sectionLabel}** Every pyrotechnic
distress signal carried on a ship shall be withdrawn from service at the
latest four years after its date of manufacture.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-218, s. 34
:::

### [Survival Craft VHF Radiotelephone Apparatus]{.HTitleText2} {#h-518986 .Subheading}

- **[[124]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-124
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every survival craft VHF
  radiotelephone apparatus carried on a ship shall meet the requirements
  of sections 222 and 232 of the [Navigation Safety Regulations,
  2020](/eng/regulations/SOR-2020-216).

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Every survival craft VHF radiotelephone apparatus
  carried on a ship shall be tested in accordance with the requirements
  of subsection 239(3) of the [Navigation Safety Regulations,
  2020](/eng/regulations/SOR-2020-216).

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-218, s. 34
- SOR/2000-261, s. 21
- [SOR/2020-216, s. 403](#1263764-1264081){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1263764-1264081.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-124-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 124"){.PITLink}
:::

### [SARTs]{.HTitleText2} {#h-518992 .Subheading}

- **[[125]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-125
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every SART carried on a ship shall
  meet the requirements of sections 222 and 231 of the [Navigation
  Safety Regulations, 2020](/eng/regulations/SOR-2020-216).

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Every SART carried on a ship shall be inspected and
  tested in accordance with the requirements of subsection 239(2) of the
  [Navigation Safety Regulations, 2020](/eng/regulations/SOR-2020-216).

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-218, s. 34
- SOR/2000-261, s. 21
- [SOR/2020-216, s. 404](#1263766-1264085){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1263766-1264085.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-125-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 125"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Class II EPIRBs]{.HTitleText2} {#h-518998 .Subheading}

- **[[126]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-126 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  Class II EPIRB carried on a ship shall meet the requirements of
  sections 222 and 228 to 230 of the [Navigation Safety Regulations,
  2020](/eng/regulations/SOR-2020-216).

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} A Class II EPIRB carried on a ship shall be inspected
  and tested in accordance with the requirements of subsection 239(1) of
  the [Navigation Safety Regulations,
  2020](/eng/regulations/SOR-2020-216).

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-218, s. 34
- SOR/2000-261, s. 21
- [SOR/2020-216, s. 405](#1263768-1264089){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1263768-1264089.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-126-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 126"){.PITLink}
:::

**[[127]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-127 .sectionLabel}** [\[Repealed,
SOR/2023-257, s. 479\]]{.Repealed}

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-127-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 127"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Lifejackets]{.HTitleText2} {#h-519008 .Subheading}

**[[127.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-127.1 .sectionLabel}** Every lifejacket
carried on a ship shall be readily accessible for immediate use and
stowed in a location that is conspicuously marked.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2004-26, s. 28
:::

### [Signs]{.HTitleText2} {#h-519012 .Subheading}

- **[[128]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-128
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Signs that indicate the location of
  survival craft, launching devices, emergency equipment, muster
  stations or embarkation stations and that provide directions to muster
  or embarkation stations shall

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} be clearly visible under emergency lighting
    conditions; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} use

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} in the case of an existing ship, words in English
      and French or symbols; and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a new ship, symbols.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Symbols shall be those set out in International
  Maritime Organization Resolution A.760(18), adopted on November 4,
  1993 and entitled Symbols Related to Life-Saving Appliances and
  Arrangements, as amended from time to time.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} Where symbols referred to in subsection (2) require
  the use of words, the words shall be in English and French.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-218, s. 34
- SOR/2004-26, s. 29
:::

### [Launching Devices]{.HTitleText2} {#h-519023 .Subheading}

**[[129]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-129 .sectionLabel}** Launching devices shall
meet the requirements set out

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} in the case of an existing ship,

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} in Part I of Schedule IX, or

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} in the LSA Code and TP 14475; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a new ship, in the LSA Code and TP
  14475.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-218, s. 34
- [SOR/2023-257, s. 480](#1431377-1435952){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1431377-1435952.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-129-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 129"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Securing and Packing of Lifeboat, Rescue Boat and Emergency Boat Equipment]{.HTitleText2} {#h-519029 .Subheading}

- **[[130]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-130
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), all
  equipment that is carried on a lifeboat, rescue boat or emergency boat
  shall be

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} secured in the boat by lashings, stowed in lockers
    or compartments or secured to brackets or other similar mountings;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} secured so that it does not interfere with
    procedures for abandoning ship; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} packed in as light and compact a form as is
    practicable.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} So that they are readily available for immediate use
  in fending off, boat-hooks shall not be secured.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-218, s. 34
:::

### [Lifebuoys and Lifebuoy Equipment]{.HTitleText2} {#h-519038 .Subheading}

- **[[131]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-131
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Lifebuoys and lifebuoy equipment
  shall meet the requirements of Schedule XIV.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Every lifebuoy carried on a ship shall be marked, in
  letters that are in a colour that contrasts with that of the lifebuoy
  and are not less than 100 mm in height, with the ship's name and port
  of registry.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-218, s. 34
:::

### [Means of Embarkation into Survival Craft]{.HTitleText2} {#h-519044 .Subheading}

**[[132]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-132 .sectionLabel}** Means of embarkation
into survival craft shall meet the requirements set out in Schedule VI.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-218, s. 34
:::

### [Muster Stations and Embarkation Stations]{.HTitleText2} {#h-519048 .Subheading}

**[[133]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-133 .sectionLabel}** Every new ship that is
a passenger ship shall have muster stations that

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} are in the vicinity of, and permit ready access by
  passengers to, the embarkation stations; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} subject to section 135, provide a clear area of at
  least 1 m^2^ for every four passengers assigned to that station for
  marshalling and instruction.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-218, s. 34
:::

**[[134]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-134 .sectionLabel}** Every new ship that is
a passenger ship shall have embarkation stations each of which provides

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} where a marine evacuation system is used, a clear
  area with enough space to allow a continuous and unencumbered flow of
  passengers from the muster station to the head of the evacuation
  system; or

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} in any other case and subject to section 135, a clear
  area of at least 1 m^2^ for every two persons to be embarked into
  survival craft from that station.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-218, s. 34
- SOR/2001-179, s. 76(F)
:::

**[[135]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-135 .sectionLabel}** Where a muster station
and an embarkation station share a common area on a new ship that is a
passenger ship, the common area shall provide at least 1 m^2^ for every
four persons to be mustered there and embarked into survival craft from
there, in addition to the space required to launch the survival craft.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-218, s. 34
:::

**[[136]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-136 .sectionLabel}** Every embarkation
station on a new ship that is a cargo ship shall have an area of at
least 1 m^2^ for every two persons to be embarked into survival craft
from that station.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-218, s. 34
:::

**[[137]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-137 .sectionLabel}** Muster and embarkation
stations for davit-launched survival craft on new ships shall be
arranged so as to enable persons on stretchers to be placed in survival
craft.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-218, s. 34
:::

**[[138]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-138 .sectionLabel}** Every muster station
and every embarkation station shall be

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} readily accessible from accommodation and service
  areas; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} adequately illuminated, with lighting capable of
  being supplied from the ship's emergency electrical power source.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-218, s. 34
:::

- **[[139]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-139
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every approach route to a muster
  station or an embarkation station, including alley-ways, stairways and
  exits, shall be adequately illuminated, with lighting capable of being
  supplied from the ship's emergency electrical power source.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Every ship shall be capable of supplying lighting to
  illuminate, during the preparation and launching of survival craft,
  the survival craft, their launching devices and the area of water into
  which they are to be launched.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-218, s. 34
:::

### [Stowage and Handling of Survival Craft]{.HTitleText2} {#h-519078 .Subheading}

#### [General Requirements]{.HTitleText3} {#h-519079 .Subheading}

**[[140]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-140 .sectionLabel}** Where davits are
required for lifeboats, rescue boats or emergency boats, a separate set
of davits shall be provided for each boat.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-218, s. 34
:::

**[[141]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-141 .sectionLabel}** A survival craft under
launching devices shall be capable of being launched with its full
complement and equipment, under 10° of trim and listing 20°.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-218, s. 34
:::

- **[[142]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-142
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where a life raft or an inflatable
  rescue platform is carried in such a position that it may be lost or
  damaged by weather or another cause, it shall be secured with a
  lashing that incorporates a senhouse slip, hydrostatic release or
  other quick-release device.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Every ship that is under 25 m in length shall carry
  its life rafts and inflatable rescue platforms

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} placed in deep chocks, without lashings, so as to
    float free if the ship sinks; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} secured by a lashing fitted with a hydrostatic
    release unit.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-218, s. 34
- SOR/2002-122, s. 7
:::

- **[[143]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-143
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Survival craft that require
  launching devices shall be stowed as close to accommodation and
  service areas as possible.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Launching stations shall be located so that survival
  craft may be launched

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} safely, taking into account the clearance from the
    propeller and steeply overhanging portions of the hull; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} insofar as it is possible, down the straight side
    of the ship, unless the survival craft are specially designed for
    free-fall launching.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} Where launching stations are positioned forward, they
  shall be located abaft the collision bulkhead in a sheltered position.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} The preparation and handling of survival craft at any
  one launching station shall not interfere with the prompt handling of
  any other survival craft at any other station.

- [(5)]{.lawlabel} Every survival craft shall be stowed

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} as near the waterline as is safe and practicable;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} so that, when in the embarkation position, it is
    not less than 2 m above the waterline when the ship is loaded with
    its full complement and equipment, under 10° of trim and listing by
    the lesser of 20° and the angle at which the ship's weatherdeck edge
    becomes submerged;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} in a state of continuous readiness so that two crew
    members may carry out preparation for embarking and launching in
    less than five minutes; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} in such a position as to prevent flooding by any
    discharge from the ship when the survival craft is being lowered to
    the water.

- [(6)]{.lawlabel} Paragraph (5)(b) does not apply to an inflatable life
  raft that does not require launching devices.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-218, s. 34
- SOR/2004-253, s. 4(F)
:::

#### [Lifeboats]{.HTitleText3} {#h-519108 .Subheading}

- **[[144]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-144
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Lifeboats for lowering down the side
  of the ship shall be stowed

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} on ships under 80 m in length, as far forward of
    the propeller as is practicable;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} on cargo ships 80 m or over but under 120 m in
    length, so that the after end of the lifeboat is not less than the
    length of the lifeboat forward of the propeller; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} on passenger ships 80 m or over in length and cargo
    ships 120 m or over in length, so that the after end of the lifeboat
    is not less than 1.5 times the length of the lifeboat forward of the
    propeller.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Lifeboats in their stowed location shall be protected
  from damage by heavy seas insofar as it is practicable.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} Lifeboats shall be stowed attached to their launching
  devices.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} Lifeboats shall be capable of being launched, where
  necessary utilizing painters, with the ship making headway at speeds
  of up to five knots in calm water.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-218, s. 34
:::

#### [Life Rafts]{.HTitleText3} {#h-519119 .Subheading}

- **[[145]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-145 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Life
  rafts shall be stowed so as to permit manual release from their
  securing arrangements.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Life rafts that are designed to be davit-launched
  shall be

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} stowed within reach of their lifting hooks; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} provided with a means of transfer that is not
    rendered inoperable by

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the ship's motion,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} a power failure,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} a list of 20° of the ship, or

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} a trim of 10° of the ship.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-218, s. 34
:::

- **[[146]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-146 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} On a
  passenger ship, every life raft that is boarded from a location on
  deck that is more than 4 m above the waterline of the ship in its
  lightest seagoing condition shall be davit-launched.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (1) does not apply in respect of an
  existing ship that is a Class II, Class III or Class IV ship referred
  to in

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} subparagraph 10(d)(ii);

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} subparagraph 11(c)(ii);

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} subparagraph 12(b)(ii);

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} subparagraph 14(d)(ii); or

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} paragraph 16(1)(d).

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} For greater certainty,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a Class III ship that, under section 15.1, complies
    with the requirements applicable to a Class IV ship, is deemed to be
    a Class IV ship for the purposes of subsection (2); and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a Class IV ship that, under section 16.1, complies
    with the requirements applicable to a Class V ship, is deemed to be
    a Class V ship for the purposes of subsection (2).

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-218, s. 34
- [SOR/2023-257, s. 481](#1431379-1435956){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1431379-1435956.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-146-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 146"){.PITLink}
:::

- **[[147]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-147
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), if a life
  raft on a ship that is not a passenger ship is stowed more than 4 m
  above the waterline of the ship in its lightest seagoing condition, it
  shall be davit-launched unless it

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} has a mass of not more than 185 kg; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} is stowed for launching directly from the stowed
    position, from which it may be safely launched against a trim of 10°
    and a list of 20°.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the ship carries life rafts for more than 200 per
  cent of the complement, those life rafts in excess of 200 per cent
  need not be davit-launched.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-218, s. 34
- SOR/2001-179, s. 47
:::

#### [Rescue Boats and Emergency Boats]{.HTitleText3} {#h-519148 .Subheading}

- **[[148]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-148
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Rescue boats and emergency boats
  shall be stowed

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in a state of continuous readiness and capable of
    being launched in less than five minutes; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in a location suitable for launching and recovery.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} A rescue boat that is also a lifeboat shall meet the
  requirements of section 144.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} Every rescue boat, when loaded with its full
  complement and equipment, shall be capable of being hoisted at a rate
  of not less than 0.3 m/s.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} Every davit-launched rescue boat or emergency boat,
  when it is installed for the first time on a ship, shall be tested to
  ensure that it may be safely launched

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} from the ship when the rescue boat or emergency
    boat is loaded with a mass equal to 110 per cent of the total of its
    own mass and that of its full complement and equipment;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} from a height of not more than 1 m above the water
    when the rescue boat or emergency boat is in its light condition, is
    suspended from its release mechanism at that height and is then
    released; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} from a height of not more than 1 m above the water
    when the rescue boat or emergency boat is loaded as described in
    paragraph (a), is suspended from its release mechanism at that
    height and is then released.

- [(5)]{.lawlabel} For the purpose of the test referred to in subsection
  (4), each member of the complement is assumed to have a mass of 75 kg.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-218, s. 34
- [SOR/2023-257, s. 482](#1431381-1435957){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1431381-1435957.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-148-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 148"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Stabilizers]{.HTitleText3} {#h-519157 .Subheading}

- **[[149]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-149
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where the survival craft of a ship
  are stowed in such a location that they may be damaged by the ship's
  stabilizer wings, the ship shall be equipped with a means of bringing
  the stabilizer wings inboard that may be operated

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} from the navigating bridge; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} by the ship's emergency source of power.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Indicators that are capable of being operated by the
  ship's emergency source of power shall be provided on the navigating
  bridge to show the position of the stabilizer wings.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-218, s. 34
:::

- **1** [\[Repealed, SOR/2023-257, s. 486\]]{.Repealed}

#### [Approved Boat Equipment]{.HTitleText2} {#approved-boat-equipment .SchedHeadL2}

- - **2** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The equipment set out in column I of the

  - [(2)]{.lawlabel} The equipment set out in column I of the table to
    this subsection is the equipment required, in addition to the
    equipment set out in column I of the table to subsection 2(1), for
    an approved boat with inflatable compartments carried on a ship
    engaged on a voyage set out in the heading of the applicable column
    of columns II and III and shall be carried in the quantity and in
    conformity with the requirements set out in that applicable column.

    <figure id="519333">
    <table
    class="table table-hover table-striped tablePointsize8 topdouble topbot">

    </tr>

    <td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom top">1</td>
    <td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom top" scope="row">buoyant safety
    knife</td>
    <td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom top">1</td>

    <td class="noBorderBottom top">1</td>
    </tr>
    <tr>
    <td class="noBorderRight top">4</td>
    <td class="noBorderRight top" scope="row">repair kit in a watertight
    container for repairing punctures</td>

  - [(3)]{.lawlabel} The buoyant oars shall

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} be sufficient in number to enable the complement
      to make headway in calm seas; and

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} each be provided with thole pins, crutches or
      equivalent arrangements that are attached to the approved boat by
      lanyards or chains.

  - [(4)]{.lawlabel} The compass shall be in a binnacle that

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} has a means of illumination; and

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} is fitted at the steering position.

  - [(5)]{.lawlabel} The sea anchor shall be fitted with a hawser and a
    tripping line that is not less than 10 m in length, both the hawser
    and the line being strong enough to withstand all sea conditions.

  - [(6)]{.lawlabel} The buoyant line shall be not less than 50 m in
    length and strong enough to tow the largest life raft carried on the
    ship at a speed of at least two knots when the life raft is loaded
    with its full complement and equipment.

  - [(7)]{.lawlabel} The searchlight shall be capable of

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} illuminating, for a period of six hours at night,
      a light-coloured object that has a width of 18 m and is at a
      distance of 180 m from the searchlight; and

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} working for at least three hours continuously.

- **3. and 4** [\[Repealed, SOR/2001-179, s. 51\]]{.Repealed}

#### [Suitable Boat Equipment]{.HTitleText2} {#suitable-boat-equipment .SchedHeadL2}

- **5** Suitable boat equipment consists of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} buoyant oars that are

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} sufficient in number to enable the complement to
      make headway in calm seas, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} each provided with thole pins, crutches or
      equivalent arrangements that are attached to the suitable boat by
      lanyards or chains;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} one buoyant bailer;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2001-179, s. 53\]]{.Repealed}

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} one painter that is placed at the forward end and
    attached to a release device;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} one buoyant line that is not less than 50 m in
    length and strong enough to tow a life raft that is loaded with 50
    persons and its equipment;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} one watertight electric flashlight suitable for
    Morse signalling, and, in a watertight container, one spare set of
    batteries and one spare bulb for the flashlight;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2001-179, s. 53\]]{.Repealed}

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} two buoyant rescue quoits, each attached to not
    less than 30 m of buoyant line;

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} if the ship is engaged on a voyage other than a
    sheltered waters voyage, thermal protective aids for two persons or
    10 per cent of the complement, whichever is greater;

  - [(j)]{.lawlabel} one safety boat-hook;

  - [(k)]{.lawlabel} if the boat is rigid, one knife or hatchet; and

  - [(l)]{.lawlabel} if the boat has inflatable compartments,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} one buoyant safety knife,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} one manually operated pump or bellows, and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} one repair kit in a watertight container for
      repairing punctures.

- **6** [\[Repealed, SOR/2023-257, s. 491\]]{.Repealed}

- **7** [\[Repealed, SOR/2001-179, s. 54\]]{.Repealed}

#### [First Aid Kits]{.HTitleText2} {#first-aid-kits .SchedHeadL2}

- - **8** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A first aid kit that is carried on a survival
    craft consists, at a minimum, of

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} 32 adhesive absorbent dressings, each 7.5 cm ×
      2.2 cm and individually wrapped;

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} four bandage gauzes, each 5 cm × 4.6 m;

    - [(c)]{.lawlabel} eight compress bandages, each 10 cm × 10 cm with
      90-cm gauze tabs;

    - [(d)]{.lawlabel} two sterile abdominal pads, each 15.2 cm × 20.3
      cm;

    - [(e)]{.lawlabel} six triangular, white muslin bandages, folded and
      compressed, each 91 cm × 96.5 cm × 137 cm;

    - [(f)]{.lawlabel} 10 sterile eye pads, each 4.69 cm × 6.98 cm;

    - [(g)]{.lawlabel} 120 mL of extra-ocular ophthalmic irrigating
      solution in an unbreakable bottle that shows a drug identification
      number and an expiry date;

    - [(h)]{.lawlabel} one unbreakable plastic eyewash cup;

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} one wire splint, 9.5 cm × 60 cm;

    - [(j)]{.lawlabel} 20 individual packs of ammonia inhalant;

    - [(k)]{.lawlabel} 20 pads, impregnated with povidone and iodine,
      that show an expiry date;

    - [(l)]{.lawlabel} one copy of Pocket Guide to Emergency First Aid,
      in English and French, published by St. John Ambulance;

    - [(m)]{.lawlabel} a waterproof contents list and instruction sheet
      in English and French;

    - [(n)]{.lawlabel} six safety pins;

    - [(o)]{.lawlabel} one pair of stainless steel bandage scissors; and

    - [(p)]{.lawlabel} one roll of waterproof adhesive tape, 2.5 cm ×
      4.5 m.

  - [(2)]{.lawlabel} The first aid kit shall be placed in a container
    that is

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} watertight;

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} resealable;

    - [(c)]{.lawlabel} fitted with a gasket to ensure a tight seal; and

    - [(d)]{.lawlabel} made of a rigid plastic that is capable of
      withstanding temperatures of -30°C, such as acrylonitrile
      butadienestyrene (ABS) or high-impact polystyrene (HIPS).
